# Insight checklist

The Phase 1 inventory was completed before cuts. An independent reviewer checked the inventory and compared reader takeaways during the initial pass, but missed local context/reference losses identified by the user. Those historical conclusions are retained below, not presented as renewed approval. Equivalent repeated entries may share one retained witness. Locations refer to the current copy.

**961 initial inventory entries retained; 18 / 18 user-requested restorations verified against the original wording and displayed copy.**

## Initial inventories and reader comparisons

### Introduction — Introduction

- [x] **Intro-I01 · claim:** Entropic evolution is portrayed as increasing complexity, interdependency, feedback, chaos, and specificity. — [introduction.md:1](introduction.md#L1).
- [x] **Intro-I02 · example:** Caves-to-cities development illustrates human cumulative creativity; chimpanzee noninnovation across generations is contrasted using the ratchet-effect citation. — [introduction.md:6](introduction.md#L6). Sources: [https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratchet_effect#Cultural_anthropology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratchet_effect#Cultural_anthropology).
- [x] **Intro-I03 · caveat:** The proposed perception ontology is an amateur synthesis with riskable predictions and practical reframing, not expert authority or guaranteed correctness. — [introduction.md:8](introduction.md#L8).
- [x] **Intro-I04 · citation:** Wittgenstein Tractatus 6.522 states that the inexpressible shows itself as the mystical. — [introduction.md:10](introduction.md#L10).
- [x] **Intro-I05 · claim:** Ethics, aesthetics, and love are presented as fully comprehensible outside language; finite communication cannot convey their entire embodied meaning. — [introduction.md:14](introduction.md#L14).
- [x] **Intro-I06 · definition:** Compression preserves contextually important structure while leaving other characteristics out. — [introduction.md:16](introduction.md#L16).
- [x] **Intro-I07 · mechanism:** Language transports fleeting experiences into interpretable symbols but compression cannot retain their full experiential richness. — [introduction.md:16](introduction.md#L16).
- [x] **Intro-I08 · definition:** Knowledge is information encoded in a form that can shape perception. — [introduction.md:18](introduction.md#L18).
- [x] **Intro-I09 · example:** Books, television, conversations, and diagrams are surface expressions of knowledge with two deeper informational layers. — [introduction.md:18](introduction.md#L18).
- [x] **Intro-I10 · mechanism:** Externalizing deeper knowledge compresses it: richness is lost while portability, stability, and shareability are gained; knowledge and feeling cannot be compressed completely. — [introduction.md:18](introduction.md#L18).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Entropic evolution increases interdependence and specificity; human cumulative creativity calls for a perceptual explanation.
- This is a provisional amateur synthesis with risky predictions, not a claim of solved truth or authority.
- Wittgenstein marks lived ethics/aesthetics/love as exceeding finite language.
- Language compresses contextually important structure, trading richness for portable, stable, shareable perceptual knowledge.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Human creativity and cumulative specificity motivate an ontology of perception and its consequences.
- The ontology is an amateur synthesis with riskable predictions, not a claim of certainty or solved problems.
- Language shares experience through lossy compression, leaving important experience ineffable.
- Knowledge is information encoded to shape perception; deeper layers gain portability and stability by losing richness when externalized.

### 1 — The Structural Ontology

### 1.1 — The Compression Gradient

- [x] **1.1-I01 · citation:** Maeterlinck’s complete epigraph compares verbalization to recovering an impoverished fragment while the original richness persists. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:1](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L1).
- [x] **1.1-I02 · caveat:** Author note requires three clearly bounded knowledge forms while permitting compression variation within each. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:12](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L12).
- [x] **1.1-I03 · claim:** Externalizing deeper knowledge compresses it; Wittgenstein’s show/say limit motivates the stack. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:14](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L14).
- [x] **1.1-I04 · caveat:** Retain the braced diagram placeholder. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:17](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L17).
- [x] **1.1-I05 · definition:** Embodied knowledge is bodily and below immediate conscious awareness: gut feelings, tacit knowledge, physiological baselines, and implicit experiential structuring. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:19](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L19).
- [x] **1.1-I06 · definition:** Representational knowledge comprises internally inspectable or manipulable images, simulations, metaphors, concepts, and models, whether implicit or explicit. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:19](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L19).
- [x] **1.1-I07 · definition:** Notational knowledge comprises stabilized, shareable external writing, diagrams, equations, and labels. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:19](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L19).
- [x] **1.1-I08 · definition:** Compression gradience is movement among the three layers: upward makes legible but loses richness; downward reconstructs richer representation or embodiment. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:21](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L21).
- [x] **1.1-I09 · example:** Putting a felt connection to God into words loses substantial experiential richness; externalization trades it for practicality. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:21](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L21).
- [x] **1.1-I10 · caveat:** Downward reconstruction cannot perfectly recover the original; one belief can remain one belief while traversing differently expressed forms. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:21](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L21).
- [x] **1.1-I11 · mechanism:** Layer influence is bidirectional: embodiment yields metaphor, metaphor changes posture/action, and sentences change models and notation. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:23](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L23).
- [x] **1.1-I12 · claim:** Decompression usually takes longer than compression because internal richness must be reconstructed; notation-to-model-to-tacit-knowing exemplifies this route. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:23](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L23).
- [x] **1.1-I13 · claim:** Representational activity is required for understanding but may be bypassed in embodied-to-notational cached outputs. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:25](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L25).
- [x] **1.1-I14 · citation:** Barsalou’s perceptual-symbol and simulation account rejects amodal semantic symbols; perceptual, motor, and introspective reenactment motivates inner-model comprehension. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:25](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L25).
- [x] **1.1-I15 · citation:** Johnson-Laird’s mental-model account describes reasoning through constructing/manipulating structurally analogous internal representations rather than static formal language rules. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:25](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L25).
- [x] **1.1-I16 · example:** “Hi, how are you?” can be automatic; if it activates a live representation it becomes comprehension rather than merely cached output. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:25](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L25).
- [x] **1.1-I17 · claim:** Thought and language are separable: wordless images and visual, spatial, motor models can be held, inspected, run, and understood. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:27](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L27).
- [x] **1.1-I18 · definition:** A representation is anything internally runnable; language counts only when it evokes a runnable model. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:27](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L27).
- [x] **1.1-I19 · example:** “invariance under rotation” unfolds into simulations for a knowledgeable reader but remains dead notation without corresponding representation/embodiment. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:27](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L27).
- [x] **1.1-I20 · claim:** Images carry structure directly whereas language is more lossy; language labels parts, creates handles such as “feedback loop,” makes intuition inspectable, shapes attention, and supports abstraction. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:27](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L27).
- [x] **1.1-I21 · caveat:** Retain author transition note asking what precedes the layers and introducing the field as the thing in the middle. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:29](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L29).
- [x] **1.1-I22 · caveat:** Retain exact frontmatter summary placeholder and draft status; author scaffolding is protected. — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:1](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L1).
- [x] **1.1-I23 · citation:** Original citation/link supports: Maurice Maeterlinck — [sections/the-compression-gradient.md:1](sections/the-compression-gradient.md#L1). Sources: [https://www.goodreads.com/quotes/698660-as-soon-as-we-put-something-into-words-we-devalue](https://www.goodreads.com/quotes/698660-as-soon-as-we-put-something-into-words-we-devalue).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Knowledge has distinct embodied, internally runnable representational, and shareable notational forms; compression varies within them.
- Upward externalization trades richness for legibility; downward reconstruction is slower and cannot perfectly recover a source state.
- Understanding requires a live representational model, unlike merely cached utterances; Barsalou and Johnson-Laird support this account.
- Thought can be wordless; language is a lossy interface that labels, chunks, directs attention, and supports abstraction rather than itself being understanding.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Knowledge has embodied, representational, and notational forms; externalizing increases legibility while losing richness.
- Compression and decompression work bidirectionally, but decompression reconstructs imperfectly and usually takes longer.
- Actual understanding requires an internally runnable representation, unlike cached automatic notation.
- Language is one representational medium/interface, not thought itself; it labels, segments, makes explicit, directs attention, and compresses.

### 1.2 — The Field

- [x] **1.2-I01 · definition:** Field: active momentary configuration of priors, bodily state, context, attention, targets and actions; mobile relevance frame for mind-body-environment. — [sections/the-field.md:1](sections/the-field.md#L1).
- [x] **1.2-I02 · definition:** Priors are probability distributions in a generative model; field is their momentary instantiation, while priors, dependency tree and weights form its substrate. — [sections/the-field.md:1](sections/the-field.md#L1).
- [x] **1.2-I03 · definition:** Field properties are emergent legible criteria/characteristics, not added objects; input includes both raw sensation and organized objects such as dogs or mathematical objects. — [sections/the-field.md:1](sections/the-field.md#L1).
- [x] **1.2-I04 · caveat:** Author treats field as consciousness’s instantiated object, formally modelable as a mathematical object using borrowed qualia formalism. — [sections/the-field.md:10](sections/the-field.md#L10).
- [x] **1.2-I05 · claim:** Phenomenal properties correspond to mathematical structural properties; STV symmetry maps to pleasantness and author identifies its measured object with the field. — [sections/the-field.md:12](sections/the-field.md#L12).
- [x] **1.2-I06 · definition:** Practical properties (constraints, variables, valence) describe inference; formal properties (symmetry, coherence, integration) are readable structural markers of the same object. — [sections/the-field.md:14](sections/the-field.md#L14).
- [x] **1.2-I07 · caveat:** Other formal properties, including coherence/unity/common organization, are proposed without technical reasoning; reader may use field metaphorically and focus on practical properties. — [sections/the-field.md:16](sections/the-field.md#L16).
- [x] **1.2-I08 · claim:** Substrate realism assumes underlying neural, bioelectric-memory and synaptic patterns influence phenomenology; priors persist and regenerate reliably across contexts. — [sections/the-field.md:18](sections/the-field.md#L18).
- [x] **1.2-I09 · caveat:** Whether specified physical mechanisms are necessary for consciousness is explicitly unanswered; further substrate discussion deferred to 1.5. — [sections/the-field.md:18](sections/the-field.md#L18).
- [x] **1.2-I10 · definition:** Field is continuously active; variables change predictions, constraints bind what fits, and fit distance measures felt proximity of input to field criteria. — [sections/the-field.md:20](sections/the-field.md#L20).
- [x] **1.2-I11 · definition:** Other simultaneous field properties include felt-possible actions, targets and valence: positive, negative or neutral experiential tone. — [sections/the-field.md:20](sections/the-field.md#L20).
- [x] **1.2-I12 · definition:** Precision weighting is estimated reliability/confidence in priors or sensory evidence, determining updating influence, attention and trust. — [sections/the-field.md:22](sections/the-field.md#L22).
- [x] **1.2-I13 · mechanism:** Precision is conditioned by past reliability in context but rapidly reweights with current input; includes sensory, preference/target and policy directions. — [sections/the-field.md:22](sections/the-field.md#L22).
- [x] **1.2-I14 · example:** Danger conditioning raises default threat precision; detected threat weights survival criteria over logical ones, changing fit tests without rebuilding the whole field. — [sections/the-field.md:22](sections/the-field.md#L22).
- [x] **1.2-I15 · definition:** Prior preferences are distributions over preferred future outcomes; target is currently prioritized outcome; policies are action sequences selected partly for reaching it. — [sections/the-field.md:24](sections/the-field.md#L24).
- [x] **1.2-I16 · mechanism:** Relative preference weights select the dominant target; target precision is rigidity of holding it. Targets coordinate policies across short and long horizons; non-dominant targets remain influential. — [sections/the-field.md:24](sections/the-field.md#L24).
- [x] **1.2-I17 · example:** Conditioned survival target can supersede social/long-term aims which persist and influence behavior after survival fulfillment. — [sections/the-field.md:24](sections/the-field.md#L24).
- [x] **1.2-I18 · caveat:** Nested cells, tissue, organs, prior clusters and nervous system can pursue concurrent targets; relevant target means dominant target or synthesis organizing attention/action. — [sections/the-field.md:26](sections/the-field.md#L26).
- [x] **1.2-I19 · example:** Conscious desire for relationship may coexist with protective embodiment making avoidance more available than connection, yielding mixed responses. — [sections/the-field.md:26](sections/the-field.md#L26).
- [x] **1.2-I20 · claim:** Sensory precision weights trusted evidence; target precision controls preferred-outcome tightness and acceptable outcome range; context/interpretation sets dominance separately. — [sections/the-field.md:28](sections/the-field.md#L28).
- [x] **1.2-I21 · definition:** Policy precision gamma, attributed to dopamine, describes decisiveness between action courses; uniformly low values are likened to freezing. — [sections/the-field.md:30](sections/the-field.md#L30).
- [x] **1.2-I22 · definition:** State-transition precision omega, attributed to noradrenaline, describes expected context stability; inverse is volatility. — [sections/the-field.md:30](sections/the-field.md#L30).
- [x] **1.2-I23 · caveat:** Framework generally uses precision collectively for factor weighting while acknowledging distinct interpretation/action commitment mechanisms. — [sections/the-field.md:32](sections/the-field.md#L32).
- [x] **1.2-I24 · definition:** Default field is foundational embodied configuration instantiated without explicitness by deep priors such as thingness, experiential center and personhood. — [sections/the-field.md:34](sections/the-field.md#L34).
- [x] **1.2-I25 · claim:** Default feels like reality because deep priors structure ongoing experience and compress theoretical sensory totality before perceptual organization. — [sections/the-field.md:34](sections/the-field.md#L34).
- [x] **1.2-I26 · claim:** Literature’s abstract slow-changing high-level priors are framework’s deep trunk; fast-updating sensory low-level priors lie outward. Trunk placement does not invert the hierarchy. — [sections/the-field.md:36](sections/the-field.md#L36).
- [x] **1.2-I27 · citation:** REBUS relaxation of high-level/deepest precision is used to interpret psychedelic effects as acting on trunk priors. — [sections/the-field.md:36](sections/the-field.md#L36).
- [x] **1.2-I28 · example:** Water-bottle interpretation branches depend on thingness plus other priors such as survival-resource, which themselves have distinct dependencies. — [sections/the-field.md:38](sections/the-field.md#L38).
- [x] **1.2-I29 · definition:** Innate evolutionarily ingrained homeostatic priors predict physiological needs such as temperature and provide a difficult-to-override survival floor. — [sections/the-field.md:38](sections/the-field.md#L38).
- [x] **1.2-I30 · example:** Energy budget changes interpretation and affordances: same bruised apple becomes food during depletion or discarded without resource need. — [sections/the-field.md:38](sections/the-field.md#L38).
- [x] **1.2-I31 · claim:** Allen/Tsakiris “first prior” is stable embodied self-awareness grounded in regulation; default is reference/dependency base not easily bypassed. — [sections/the-field.md:38](sections/the-field.md#L38).
- [x] **1.2-I32 · definition:** Active inference acts to make input match the model; default priors structure sensation and behavior expresses or fulfills predictions. — [sections/the-field.md:40](sections/the-field.md#L40).
- [x] **1.2-I33 · mechanism:** Default versus situational explicit-lens properties is a gradient; repetition embeds added properties into default embodiment, yielding expert fast perception/action. — [sections/the-field.md:40](sections/the-field.md#L40).
- [x] **1.2-I34 · example:** Switching conversation to phone activates differing listening/posture/tone versus gaze/finger/awareness priors and changes bodily action readiness. — [sections/the-field.md:43](sections/the-field.md#L43).
- [x] **1.2-I35 · claim:** Field is embodied preparation, response, movement and regulation, affecting posture, tension, attention, breathing and possibilities mostly non-consciously. — [sections/the-field.md:43](sections/the-field.md#L43).
- [x] **1.2-I36 · definition:** Prediction tree is distribution over competing interpretations and future trajectories; precision weights branches and targets modulate associated policies. — [sections/the-field.md:45](sections/the-field.md#L45).
- [x] **1.2-I37 · mechanism:** Perception usually commits to dominant posterior branch while lower-probability alternatives persist; increased posterior confidence weights associated actions toward preferred states. — [sections/the-field.md:45](sections/the-field.md#L45).
- [x] **1.2-I38 · definition:** Epistemic value is expected policy information about hidden states; favors uncertainty-reducing play/research and answers dark-room objection to pure prediction matching. — [sections/the-field.md:45](sections/the-field.md#L45).
- [x] **1.2-I39 · example:** Retain linked prediction-tree illustration with its simplified-singular caption (line 48); diagram encodes competing trajectories. — [sections/the-field.md:47](sections/the-field.md#L47).
- [x] **1.2-I40 · definition:** Prediction error is expected versus actual input, reducible by action or model updating; target error is framework-specific current-versus-preferred-state gap. — [sections/the-field.md:51](sections/the-field.md#L51).
- [x] **1.2-I41 · claim:** Prediction error concerns already observed mismatch (milliseconds/seconds); target error is described as future expected-versus-preferred outcomes (seconds/years). Related risk and PCT error-signal concepts are linked. — [sections/the-field.md:51](sections/the-field.md#L51).
- [x] **1.2-I42 · definition:** Chunking prunes unmanageable branch search by packaging separate prior structures (water bottle), dropping richness while freeing attention. — [sections/the-field.md:51](sections/the-field.md#L51).
- [x] **1.2-I43 · definition:** Dependency tree supports field configuration: deep trunk priors enable competing predicted-state branches; foundational trunk is not simply selectable. — [sections/the-field.md:53](sections/the-field.md#L53).
- [x] **1.2-I44 · example:** Water-bottle versus thermos branches share stable thingness; changing a branch is easier than changing trunk because deep updates disrupt dependent structures and threaten system stability. — [sections/the-field.md:53](sections/the-field.md#L53).
- [x] **1.2-I45 · example:** Retain linked simplified dependency-tree illustration and caption (line 56). — [sections/the-field.md:55](sections/the-field.md#L55).
- [x] **1.2-I46 · caveat:** Foundational priors include causality, time, anthropomorphism, status, reputation, conformity, threat, disgust, body ownership and self, with more basic objecthood/agency; levels differ and shared organism-survival priors are only proposed. — [sections/the-field.md:59](sections/the-field.md#L59).
- [x] **1.2-I47 · claim:** Internal representation is an object placed on field, not a total field; mostly reweights shallow branches and is cheap to apply/remove without changing foundations. — [sections/the-field.md:61](sections/the-field.md#L61).
- [x] **1.2-I48 · example:** “argument is war” introduces/reweights attacking and defending branches and activates corresponding field criteria. — [sections/the-field.md:61](sections/the-field.md#L61).
- [x] **1.2-I49 · mechanism:** Representation first tests fit against current field, then integrates/reconfigures it; repeated use can train deeper dependency structures. — [sections/the-field.md:61](sections/the-field.md#L61).
- [x] **1.2-I50 · example:** Roof-fall image fits elevation, gravity and threat priors despite negative valence; target survival and unpleasant action-guiding feeling can diverge. — [sections/the-field.md:63](sections/the-field.md#L63).
- [x] **1.2-I51 · claim:** Unpleasant fall imagery favors avoidance rather than survival-violating branches; author contrasts hypothetical pleasant imagery increasing motivation to fall. — [sections/the-field.md:63](sections/the-field.md#L63).
- [x] **1.2-I52 · mechanism:** Fantasies can internally preserve unavailable preferred-state properties; comparison extracts stable safety/agency/belonging/adventure properties and finds alternatives preserving them. — [sections/the-field.md:65](sections/the-field.md#L65).
- [x] **1.2-I53 · example:** Other-planet fantasy’s freedom, exploration, belonging and wonder can be separated from setting and pursued in ordinary environments/actions. — [sections/the-field.md:65](sections/the-field.md#L65).
- [x] **1.2-I54 · definition:** Phenomenal transparency hides representation construction so only content appears; field/goggles selectively highlight and dim what is known. — [sections/the-field.md:67](sections/the-field.md#L67).
- [x] **1.2-I55 · caveat:** Raw experience/sensory totality is theoretical chaotic high-entropy input never directly met; embodied richness is already structured by deep priors and contraction. — [sections/the-field.md:67](sections/the-field.md#L67).
- [x] **1.2-I56 · citation:** Complete Huxley reducing-valve quotation illustrates survival filtering by brain/nervous system and further symbol-system compression; no hyperlink supplies its source. — [sections/the-field.md:67](sections/the-field.md#L67).
- [x] **1.2-I57 · caveat:** Sensory totality remains an unexperienceable theoretical model; evidence is offered for filtering and overwhelm, not direct access to totality. — [sections/the-field.md:69](sections/the-field.md#L69).
- [x] **1.2-I58 · claim:** Entropic-brain/REBUS accounts suggest psychedelics increase neural entropy, relax top-down high-level/DMN constraints and permit flooding or boundary dissolution. — [sections/the-field.md:69](sections/the-field.md#L69).
- [x] **1.2-I59 · caveat:** Jhana/5-MeO-DMT can show increased entropy with less complex whiteout/cessation experience, complicating “more entropy = more flooding.” — [sections/the-field.md:69](sections/the-field.md#L69).
- [x] **1.2-I60 · example:** Schizophrenia poor sensory gating/inundation and migraine altered processing, visual hypersensitivity, lack of habituation support filtering-pathology argument. — [sections/the-field.md:69](sections/the-field.md#L69).
- [x] **1.2-I61 · claim:** TRN gates sensory relay and high gain can disconnect cortex; behavioral-throughput estimate ~10 bits/s versus at least gigabit/s inflow supports massive selection. — [sections/the-field.md:69](sections/the-field.md#L69).
- [x] **1.2-I62 · citation:** Laukkonen/Slagter predictive deconstruction describes meditation disengaging anticipatory processes toward here-and-now experience. — [sections/the-field.md:69](sections/the-field.md#L69).
- [x] **1.2-I63 · definition:** Attention directs precision across field/dependencies automatically or manually, controlling property weighting through moment weighting, layer dwelling and sculpting. — [sections/the-field.md:71](sections/the-field.md#L71).
- [x] **1.2-I64 · mechanism:** Moment weighting selects current relevance; dwelling in a layer with safety/time can retune field and produce aha/background compression-decompression; repetition reinforces structure into trunk/tacit expertise. — [sections/the-field.md:73](sections/the-field.md#L73).
- [x] **1.2-I65 · claim:** Three attention roles are mapped to literature’s state inference (moment), parameter learning (repetitions), structure learning (offline); insight alone is insufficient because practices act across levels. — [sections/the-field.md:73](sections/the-field.md#L73).
- [x] **1.2-I66 · caveat:** Experience ranges from explicit lenses to implicit amodal perception (unseen hand back/building side); awareness holds wider structured configuration while abstract cognition compresses richness into manipulable structures. — [sections/the-field.md:75](sections/the-field.md#L75).
- [x] **1.2-I67 · definition:** Slack is capacity not committed to sustaining current field, enabling prediction error without immediate defensive habitual active inference; felt safety alone seems insufficient for deep-prior updating; expanded in 2.3. — [sections/the-field.md:77](sections/the-field.md#L77).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- The field is an instantiated consciousness/configuration of priors, body, attention, context, targets and affordances, distinguished from its physical substrate and formal/practical structural properties.
- Precision dynamically weights sensory evidence, preferred outcomes, policies and expected stability; targets can conflict across nested embodied systems.
- Deep default priors structure apparent reality and support a dependency tree; prediction trees instead describe competing interpretations and trajectories, constrained by energy and epistemic value.
- Representations reweight branches, may train deeper priors, and can separate fantasy targets from their unavailable forms; coherent threat images can fit while feeling unpleasant.
- Sensory totality is theoretical, gating/entropy evidence is qualified, and attention weights, dwells and sculpts across timescales; deeper updating requires slack beyond felt safety.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- The field is the momentary instantiated configuration/object of experience, grounded in persistent priors and physical substrate, not a freestanding representation.
- Precision and attention reweight sensory evidence, preferred outcomes, and action policies; dominant targets can coexist with competing subsystem targets.
- Deep embodied priors support dependency-tree branches; representations cheaply reweight shallow interpretations and repetition can change deeper defaults.
- Prediction error differs from target error; active inference combines evidence-seeking and action with model updating rather than merely seeking predictable input.
- Sensory totality is theoretical and perception already filtered; attention acts across immediate weighting, dwelling, and long-term sculpting, while deep change needs slack.

### 1.3 — Chunking, Gestalts, and Attractors

- [x] **1.3-I01 · definition:** Chunking compresses separately integrated parts into one field-treated unit, preserving structure and deferring recovery of richness; chunks are learned reusable units reducing processing/search cost. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:1](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L1).
- [x] **1.3-I02 · mechanism:** Precision prunes dependency-tree branches into a unit, shrinking interpretations and freeing attention. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:1](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L1).
- [x] **1.3-I03 · definition:** Gestalt is presently instantiated unified whole organized from priors; a novel image can become one without prior encounter but needs existing structure. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:1](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L1).
- [x] **1.3-I04 · definition:** Barrett’s category is a situation-specific event treating different things as equivalent; dynamic categories differ from learned chunk dispositions enabling reproduction. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:1](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L1).
- [x] **1.3-I05 · example:** Retain linked chunk/gestalt image; surrounding novel-image discussion is its context. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:10](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L10).
- [x] **1.3-I06 · mechanism:** Repeated use may store novel image relational structure as chunk via repetition, stable relations and prediction-confirming successful action. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:12](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L12).
- [x] **1.3-I07 · mechanism:** Coactivation evidence for lower priors (shape, use, interactions, weight) permits higher-unit chunking; coherent organization appears as gestalt. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:14](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L14).
- [x] **1.3-I08 · example:** Water-bottle gestalt combines chunked lower-level shape and usage priors. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:14](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L14).
- [x] **1.3-I09 · example:** Mathematicians rapidly bring several knowledge elements online; expertise means calibrated chunks and whole/detail flexibility, not merely richer chunks. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:16](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L16).
- [x] **1.3-I10 · claim:** Temporal chunking binds changing input into stable-now gestalt using spatial-compression principle. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:16](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L16).
- [x] **1.3-I11 · definition:** Prior is persistent disposition stored in not-yet-understood substrate, actively regenerated for contextual field presence; stable trunk is reliable reproduction, not absence of storage or true permanence. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:16](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L16).
- [x] **1.3-I12 · caveat:** Sensory clarity can dechunk time into sensations/events; goal is not removing useful chunks but recognizing omissions and recovering parts when gestalt fails. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:16](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L16).
- [x] **1.3-I13 · definition:** Attractors are mutually reinforcing configurations repeatedly settled into, even against conscious preference; explain persistence rather than dependency support. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:18](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L18).
- [x] **1.3-I14 · mechanism:** Repeated coactivation binds larger gestalts so one part reinstates others and configuration outcompetes alternatives. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:18](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L18).
- [x] **1.3-I15 · example:** Anxiety attractor binds arousal, threat attention, catastrophe interpretation and low corrective-evidence precision; safety-target policies can reinforce unwanted anxiety. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:18](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L18).
- [x] **1.3-I16 · caveat:** Attractor is not a singular prior but region/basin of structure reinstantiating similar fields; target preference need not match attraction. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:18](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L18).
- [x] **1.3-I17 · definition:** Attractor basin is range of states returning to configuration; width captures many starting states, depth resists disturbance after entry. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:20](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L20).
- [x] **1.3-I18 · example:** Partial arousal plus threat can reinstate full anxiety even without initially complete anxiety. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:20](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L20).
- [x] **1.3-I19 · mechanism:** Repetition, bodily reinforcement, environment regularity and policy enactment widen/deepen basins; corrective evidence, chunks, alternative policies and slack permit competitors. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:20](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L20).
- [x] **1.3-I20 · mechanism:** Basin thresholds permit gradual unnoticed changes followed by another pattern taking over when current self-reinforcement weakens. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:20](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L20).
- [x] **1.3-I21 · definition:** Hysteresis is path-dependent asymmetric entry/exit: small threat triggers anxiety while escape needs harder-to-maintain/perceive strong safety signals under biased evidence/action. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:20](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L20).
- [x] **1.3-I22 · example:** Retain linked hysteresis/basin diagram. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:22](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L22).
- [x] **1.3-I23 · claim:** Hysteresis is related to broader cheap many-to-one collapse versus expensive one-to-many reconstruction; embodied-to-notation is quicker than unfamiliar-notation-to-embodiment. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:24](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L24).
- [x] **1.3-I24 · definition:** Reachability is whether viable path to attractor exists under current available actions; equal width/depth does not imply equal accessibility. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:26](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L26).
- [x] **1.3-I25 · claim:** A field property/constraint is load-bearing if changing it reorganizes possible paths to configurations; conditioned barriers (including felt tone) can sustain attractors. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:26](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L26).
- [x] **1.3-I26 · caveat:** Reachability is state-specific: recognizing harmful pattern and effectively acting on recognition require distinct possibilities, with recognition likely more reachable. — [sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md:28](sections/chunking-gestalts-and-attractors.md#L28).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Chunks are reusable dispositions; a gestalt is the presently unified result, and repetition can turn novel organizations into chunks.
- Expertise flexibly opens well-calibrated chunks; temporal continuity is a re-instantiated gestalt rather than an immutable trunk.
- Attractors are self-reinforcing configurations distinct from targets and individual priors; basin width/depth, thresholds and hysteresis govern persistence.
- Reachability concerns available paths and differs from recognizing versus acting on a pattern; missing prerequisites permit only proxy gestalts.
- Neural annealing loosens substrate with added energy then integrates a new configuration, linked to Johnson and STV.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Chunks are reusable learned compression; gestalts are presently instantiated wholes, including novel ones; categories are context-dependent organizations.
- Experts use calibrated chunks and flexible access to omitted components; temporal chunking makes stable now, while persistent priors must regenerate each moment.
- Attractors are self-reinforcing recurring field configurations, not targets or singular priors; basin width/depth and hysteresis explain persistence and asymmetric entry/exit.
- Reachability concerns available action paths and differs for recognizing versus acting; missing prerequisites permit only proxy rather than genuine gestalts.
- Annealing is proposed temporary substrate energization/exploration followed by cooling/integration that may make alternative attractors reachable.

### 1.4 — Thoughts & Memories

- [x] **1.4-I01 · definition:** Folk categories are ordinary-use taxonomies rather than theoretically shaped categories; thoughts, memories, and emotions name related property clusters of gestalts, not unrelated kinds. Thought and memory are proposed to share constructive mechanisms while differing in final characteristics; emotion is deferred. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:1](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L1). Sources: [https://philarchive.org/rec/ANDADF-3?utm_source=chatgpt.com](https://philarchive.org/rec/ANDADF-3?utm_source=chatgpt.com); [https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/behavioral-and-brain-sciences/article/abs/on-the-constructive-episodic-simulation-of-past-and-future-events/4857CCE46631D0DBC017B7F45AB4028A](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/behavioral-and-brain-sciences/article/abs/on-the-constructive-episodic-simulation-of-past-and-future-events/4857CCE46631D0DBC017B7F45AB4028A).
- [x] **1.4-I02 · definition:** The framework stipulates, rather than settles metaphysically, that a thought is an attendable discrete gestalt decoupled from present input and carrying the semantics of its compressed organization. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:10](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L10).
- [x] **1.4-I03 · example:** A blue-dog thought is a discrete object absent from current physical input, carrying blue/dog semantics through language and associative images; a mental image places the thought medium in the representational layer. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:10](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L10).
- [x] **1.4-I04 · caveat:** Decoupling is not an absence of properties, field influence, or bodily effects, nor a hard boundary for the system: an anxiety-inducing thought can raise heart rate because active inference handles the gestalt like physical-world input. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:10](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L10).
- [x] **1.4-I05 · caveat:** An input-driven embodied gut feeling can generate a thought that is decoupled in the sense of not being attached or tagged onto the physical layer; the physical/mental boundary can be murky. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:12](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L12).
- [x] **1.4-I06 · mechanism:** Aboutness comes from compression organizing relevant relations and associations: water-bottle semantics concern bottles because the gestalt organizes bottle relations and associative properties. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:14](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L14).
- [x] **1.4-I07 · caveat:** Most cognitive products are not thoughts because they are not discrete attendable objects; deeply implicit amodal processing, exemplified by the back of the hand, is difficult to notice or turn into a discrete thought. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:14](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L14).
- [x] **1.4-I08 · mechanism:** Noticing a thought is not an outside privileged observation: selfhood priors activated by inference model an apparently separate observer. Observer, thinker, and doer are gestalts of the same medium as thoughts, differing in persistence and depth. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:16](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L16).
- [x] **1.4-I09 · example:** Participating in thoughts can multiply them; rumination and overthinking reinstate clusters of field configurations, as replaying a conversation late at night brings its associated state online. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:16](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L16).
- [x] **1.4-I10 · citation:** The complete Michael Levin quotation characterizes persons as massive self-persistent thought patterns across media, spawning and hosting smaller patterns, and treats data/machine and thought/thinker distinctions as names on a spectrum rather than true dichotomies. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:18](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L18).
- [x] **1.4-I11 · claim:** Within the proposed nested hierarchy, some thoughts seem more agentic or animated: stronger responses across the three layers can make a thought feel more real through embodiment or produce more complex internal simulations. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:18](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L18).
- [x] **1.4-I12 · example:** Thoughts can occupy every dependency-tree depth: a passing image is shallow in the branches, while the background sense of being a person is deep near the trunk and underpins other thoughts. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:20](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L20).
- [x] **1.4-I13 · mechanism:** Momentary attention reweights fast properties and changes thoughts through effects on the field, not directly on thought content; dwelling attention retunes the field and creates a thought/perception feedback loop. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:20](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L20).
- [x] **1.4-I14 · mechanism:** Sculpting through attention repeatedly embeds thoughts and associated field configurations in the embodied layer, increasing their availability and engraving associated characteristics. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:20](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L20).
- [x] **1.4-I15 · claim:** Thoughts and mind-objects are generated, not merely fetched: fantasy, future events, and a never-seen blue dog require construction. Priors, context, and the field constrain generation rather than permitting infinitely random content. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:22](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L22).
- [x] **1.4-I16 · definition:** Memory as a process is learning persistent dispositions that change what a system can reproduce. Recall is dynamic decompression: changing structure runs in the present to generate output. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:24](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L24).
- [x] **1.4-I17 · definition:** Conscious recollection can be a generated gestalt carrying implicit or explicit temporal semantics. Memory-like identity is a property cluster including temporality and familiarity, exemplified by breakfast images/language marked this morning. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:24](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L24). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5404722/?utm_source=chatgpt.com](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5404722/?utm_source=chatgpt.com); [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5312673/?utm_source=chatgpt.com](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5312673/?utm_source=chatgpt.com).
- [x] **1.4-I18 · example:** Déjà vu carries familiar bodily markers without matching the temporal content normally associated with past recollection: its input is present rather than the expected past-memory property cluster. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:24](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L24). Sources: [https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1111/j.1467-8721.2008.00605.x](https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1111/j.1467-8721.2008.00605.x); [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22322010/?utm_source=chatgpt.com](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22322010/?utm_source=chatgpt.com).
- [x] **1.4-I19 · claim:** What persists is a disposition toward regenerating a memory configuration, not the recalled content itself; memories are structural changes in reproduction conditions. Bike learning changes reproducible bodily action, not an isolated stored film. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:24](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L24).
- [x] **1.4-I20 · example:** Adult moths retaining learned caterpillar behavior after metamorphosis illustrate memory persistence and transfer despite a changed physical substrate. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:24](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L24). Sources: [https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0001736](https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0001736).
- [x] **1.4-I21 · claim:** Memory requires persistence and access to dispositions, but persistence is in landscapes rather than particular states: basins and field configurations result from tuned parameters, not independently context-free stored objects. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:26](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L26). Sources: [https://linkinghub.elsevier.com/retrieve/pii/S0022537166800488](https://linkinghub.elsevier.com/retrieve/pii/S0022537166800488).
- [x] **1.4-I22 · example:** The spider-tower model uses reverberating strings at hierarchical levels, with deep trunk priors at the top: dropped input is processed through them, and interacting lengths/tensions generate outputs instead of storing outputs inside strings. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:26](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L26).
- [x] **1.4-I23 · mechanism:** Changing a string or layer changes the generative structure, not a statically stored output object. Input traversal also retunes the tower through string interactions over time: use changes the producing structure itself. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:26](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L26).
- [x] **1.4-I24 · definition:** Storage remains necessary as durable data structures/algorithms embodied in synapses, connectivity, excitability, and associative engram relations. Memory is compressed dispositions; conscious active recall reconstructs from that past-derived structure. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:28](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L28).
- [x] **1.4-I25 · claim:** Decompression produces knowledge in notational, representational, or embodied form, selected by current conditions and the field rather than the original input form. The section introduces bow-tie architecture as input passing through maximum field compression. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:30](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L30).
- [x] **1.4-I26 · claim:** The isolated-storage model is contrasted with a structural model: storage need not grow linearly with memorized items, and new learning distorts old recall through cascading changes in shared structure rather than proportionally crowding old items out. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:32](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L32).
- [x] **1.4-I27 · claim:** The section argues a stored lookup table cannot generate an unstored blue dog, whereas field-constrained rules and structure can; this extends the structural-memory account to novel thoughts. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:32](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L32).
- [x] **1.4-I28 · mechanism:** Recall through current structure trades detail for usefulness under metabolic constraints: salience rather than fidelity guides reconstruction, and the field makes current perception bend what is recalled. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:34](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L34).
- [x] **1.4-I29 · example:** Moth/caterpillar memory is used to illustrate salience-preserving refitting: after substrate change, the claim is that detailed caterpillar memories are less useful while survival-relevant transfer survives through new structure. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:24](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L24); [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:34](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L34). Sources: [https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0001736](https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0001736).
- [x] **1.4-I30 · mechanism:** Forgetting is reframed as ceasing maintenance of what no longer matters: prolonged lack of recall can reduce reachability and degrade structure; long-term structural change can also make a recollection happier than the original event. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:34](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L34).
- [x] **1.4-I31 · caveat:** Salience is not goodness: trauma can be the same adaptive mechanism unpacking memories and cues within a danger frame. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:34](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L34).
- [x] **1.4-I32 · citation:** The complete Levin quotation distinguishes unavailable past events from present memory traces/engrams in mind, brain, and body that can be reinterpreted without allegiance to their previous interpretation; storage explains continuity while changed current structure explains changed recall. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:36](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L36).
- [x] **1.4-I33 · definition:** Retrieval is not denied: fluent 3×6=18 feels instant, but is described as reliable conditioned reinstatement of a deep reachable chunk with no competing branches. Fluency is easy reliable movement between configurations/dispositions whose reproduction conditions are readily met. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:38](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L38).
- [x] **1.4-I34 · example:** Differing ease of reproducing 3×6 and 7×8 is used against an equal-access static lookup-table account. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:38](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L38). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9054599/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9054599/).
- [x] **1.4-I35 · mechanism:** Recall errors cluster in related generative structures rather than arbitrary outputs: 3×6 may yield 24 rather than 156, and memory mistakes similarly land in related branches/strings rather than fantastical unconstrained alternatives. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:38](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L38). Sources: [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17176659/](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17176659/).
- [x] **1.4-I36 · mechanism:** Current mood can bend recalled memories; because generating recall retunes producing structure through propagating effects, the author claims the shift can persist after the initiating mood disappears. Apparent retrieval can therefore be indistinguishable from sophisticated generation. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:38](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L38). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10076454/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10076454/).
- [x] **1.4-I37 · definition:** Reachability is how easy a memory is to reach and enact now; strong retention can coexist with low reachability, so a well-learned language or memory may be difficult to access without losing its underlying qualities. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:40](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L40).
- [x] **1.4-I38 · mechanism:** Effortful retrieval is proposed to retune generative structure, explaining spaced repetition; easy retrieval yields little variability and little new learning. Cramming can feel effective and work briefly without deep long-term retuning, and retrieval difficulty need not imply damage. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:40](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L40).
- [x] **1.4-I39 · caveat:** Reachability does not account for all forgetting: memories can genuinely transform or be lost through active decay. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:40](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L40). Sources: [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23369831/](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23369831/).
- [x] **1.4-I40 · mechanism:** Mood, environment, and social situations change starting conditions. Generative reviews on different days widen basins and strengthen reachability across contexts, rather than only from one starting point. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:42](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L42).
- [x] **1.4-I41 · caveat:** The same basin-widening mechanism can condition trauma across contexts; producing content strengthens memory, illustrated by writing rather than merely re-reading notation. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:42](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L42).
- [x] **1.4-I42 · claim:** Culture and environment do not create basic memory mechanisms but reshape attention, information organization, interpretation, and ultimately remembered structure. Writing omits details and photography foregrounds imagery, preserving different aspects otherwise collapsed in the dependency tree. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:44](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L44).
- [x] **1.4-I43 · mechanism:** Memorizing a fact changes generative structure across all three layers, not an isolated notation node: USA-is-a-country learning alters vocal movements, internal model, and writing. A notation cue like 3×5 can receive embodied answer signals, so the change marker need not occupy the cue's layer. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:46](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L46).
- [x] **1.4-I44 · example:** Shared multiplication structure constrains candidate answers before deliberate recall, unlike arbitrary face/name associations. This accounts for related numerical mistakes, greater repetition needed for arbitrary pairs, and mnemonics supplying shared discriminating branches. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:48](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L48).
- [x] **1.4-I45 · claim:** Memory expertise parallels sensory clarity: distinguishing 3×3 from 3×4 and de-chunking a sensation both individuate fine structure, grow shared branches, and improve discrimination. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:50](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L50).
- [x] **1.4-I46 · claim:** Recall success is target/framing-dependent, often conditioned rather than universal: an exact ordered speech target makes partial recall a target error, while instructor-defined multiple-choice grading imposes binary correctness even on relationally close answers. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:52](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L52).
- [x] **1.4-I47 · caveat:** Sameness of memory is problematized by changes in bodily response, time, and social context; treating perceived content alone as identity would exclude embodied feelings that can themselves change content, including newly noticed details. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:54](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L54).
- [x] **1.4-I48 · claim:** Memories/thoughts are described as alive insofar as they respond to environmentally and bodily shaped structure and error-correct against held targets. Comparison to the original event in its totality is impossible because neither the original structure nor an objective full vantage can be recovered. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:54](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L54).
- [x] **1.4-I49 · example:** A memory formed at 18 and recalled at 19/20 with altered bodily response may count as the same under did-this-happen-to-me but not under does-it-produce-suffering; identity also involves relations, people, continuity, and location, not targets alone. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:56](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L56).
- [x] **1.4-I50 · claim:** Memory identity is organizational rather than fixed-object identity: context-sensitive disposition regeneration depends on input, environment, and mood, with dependency-tree responses affecting the field and looping back into the recalled product. — [sections/thoughts-and-memories.md:56](sections/thoughts-and-memories.md#L56).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Thoughts are discrete attended decoupled semantic gestalts, while much implicit cognition is not thought; the observer is another gestalt rather than an external vantage.
- Memory is durable generative disposition and recall reconstructs knowledge through current field/body, not a stored film; spider-string structure illustrates propagation and self-modification.
- Recall favors current salience rather than fidelity, can retune mood-biased memories, and retains lawful error patterns and fluent retrieval-like multiplication.
- Retention differs from reachability and decay still occurs; effortful spaced varied-context generation widens basins, whereas arbitrary pairings need repetition or mnemonic structure.
- Memory affects all layers, culture changes its organization, and recall success/identity depend on targets and changing embodied/contextual relations.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Thoughts, memories, and emotions are treated as related folk labels for gestalt property clusters. A thought is an attendable discrete gestalt decoupled from present input but still able to organize the field and affect the body; thinker and thought differ in depth and persistence, not medium.
- Thoughts and recall are generated under current priors, context, and field conditions. Memory is persistent learned reproductive disposition, not an isolated stored film; durable physical structure carries dispositions, and recall reconstructs knowledge in any of the three layers.
- Recall optimizes current salience rather than original fidelity, and using recall retunes the structure that generated it. New learning, mood, culture, and practices can therefore change later recall; this adaptive mechanism can also perpetuate trauma.
- Retention and present reachability differ. Effortful retrieval, spaced repetition, and context variation can strengthen and widen access, while active decay can also genuinely transform or lose memories; fluency and structured errors arise from shared generative branches.
- Memorization changes notation, representation, and embodiment together. Arbitrary associations benefit from mnemonics, expertise refines shared structure, and both recall success and memory identity depend on targets and organizational continuity rather than a universal binary or fixed object.

### 1.5 — Bow-Tie Architecture

- [x] **1.5-I01 · claim:** Survival under limited modeling capacity requires discovering lower-dimensional causes/relations that preserve what matters and using them creatively. — [sections/bow-tie-architecture.md:1](sections/bow-tie-architecture.md#L1).

- [x] **1.5-I59 · definition:** A bow tie is the dependency tree serving a selected target: framed relevant input, the most compressed commitment-relevant state, and organized downstream means. — [sections/bow-tie-architecture.md:86](sections/bow-tie-architecture.md#L86).
- [x] **1.5-I60 · example:** Become-a-doctor organizes medical-school subtargets and tiny notebook-reaching acts over a wider range than cell ion-balance. - [x] **1.5-I61 · claim:** Larger abstract targets imply richer bottlenecks retaining more invariants and larger nested generative trees; a cell's highest-order commitment state is comparatively small next to a human doctor goal. — [sections/bow-tie-architecture.md:88](sections/bow-tie-architecture.md#L88); [sections/bow-tie-architecture.md:90](sections/bow-tie-architecture.md#L90).
- [x] **1.5-I62 · definition:** The cognitive light cone is the spatial/temporal scale of the largest goal a system can pursue: an outer capacity boundary, not a single whole bow tie or the size of every concurrent process. — [sections/bow-tie-architecture.md:92](sections/bow-tie-architecture.md#L92). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13197194/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13197194/).
- [x] **1.5-I63 · mechanism:** Estimate the largest target by starting with a candidate prediction, perturbing invariant variables, and checking active restoration across disturbances; stop extending the inferred boundary when no evidence of larger-state restoration remains. — [sections/bow-tie-architecture.md:92](sections/bow-tie-architecture.md#L92).
- [x] **1.5-I64 · example:** Delayed train, dead phone, or stranger perturbations test whether nested behavior continues restoring getting-home-safe. **Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Biological and memory bow ties compress input to target-sufficient latent structure then creatively decode; perception extends this to recurrent distributed overlapping cycles rather than a single location or linear handoff.
- Slow physical structure regenerates targets; the same priors infer causes and generate perception/action, while the field is a simultaneous generated cross-section of active bow ties, not the hidden bottleneck.
- SPTs preserve target-salient relations; invariance retains organization and equivariance tracks useful changes, enabling flexible intelligence without implying moral goodness.
- Bow-tie scale varies with target/horizon and nested means; cognitive light cone bounds the largest perturbation-restored target, changes through development or scarcity, and is not one whole bow tie.
- Embodiment constrains observability, integration and controllability; Markov blanket channels limit but do not determine reach, which depends on learned structure and requisite variety.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Bow-tie architecture synthesizes compression and creative generation: many inputs converge into a smaller useful organization and branch into output, with output changing later input. Biological metabolism, semantic memory compression, predictive processing, and neural encoder/decoder models supply cited analogues rather than proving a single physical bottleneck.
- The bottleneck is a distributed, target- and timescale-relative minimum sufficient state, not a literal location. Persistent structure and learned memory reproduce targets; the same hierarchy of priors organizes inference and generation through overlapping nested bow ties without a hard cognition/body split.
- The field is the momentary instantiated synthesis of active bow ties, distinct from the hidden bottleneck. Fast inference changes current configurations while slower learning retunes structure; regenerated slow invariants constrain fast changes and support a continuous-seeming experience.
- Structure-preserving transformations combine invariant target-relevant relations with equivariant action-relevant changes. Intelligence finds different means to a preserved end and generalizes learned relations, but invariance can maintain trauma or harmful ideology and is not intrinsically good.
- Bow-tie scale depends on the selected target and observer analysis. Cognitive light cones bound the largest spatial/temporal targets demonstrated under perturbations and can grow or shrink; physical embodiment and Markov-blanket sensing/action channels constrain but do not uniquely determine that reach.

### 2.1 — The Energetic Triad

- [x] **2.1-I01 · definition:** Cost is relative limited-capacity demand and additional processing allocation, not calories burned. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:1](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L1).
- [x] **2.1-I02 · mechanism:** Finite energetic resources make compression necessary; tracking and memorizing everything is unsustainable. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:12](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L12).
- [x] **2.1-I03 · definition:** Processing handles input; integration reconfigures the field to account for input, producing felt sense when deviations are consciously noticeable. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:14](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L14).
- [x] **2.1-I04 · mechanism:** Ongoing sensory input entails continuous predicted versus actual processing demand while maintaining a workable interpretation. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:14](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L14).
- [x] **2.1-I05 · claim:** Valence correlates with precision-weighted difference between current field configuration and predictions. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:16](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L16).
- [x] **2.1-I06 · definition:** Targets are preferred predicted states pursued by varying means rather than revising the state to reality. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:16](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L16).
- [x] **2.1-I07 · mechanism:** Ordinary priors update and small errors fade; target errors persist until higher-level reset, loosening, or dropping, under hierarchical constraints such as survival. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:16](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L16).
- [x] **2.1-I08 · definition:** Integration-cost prediction error is actual versus predicted integration demand, correlated with bodily and valence spikes. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:18](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L18).
- [x] **2.1-I09 · example:** A seemingly simple math problem becomes hard without prerequisites and generally produces negative bodily and valence spikes. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:18](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L18).
- [x] **2.1-I10 · definition:** Triad: cheaper than predicted corresponds to positive valence/resonance; expected demand to neutral fit-baseline; greater demand to negative valence/friction. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:20](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L20).
- [x] **2.1-I11 · claim:** High-cost proxies are rereading/time, extra-focus effort, and tension; low-cost proxies are immediacy, freed attention, and tension release. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:22](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L22).
- [x] **2.1-I12 · example:** A lion is quickly recognized yet expensive to integrate because survival-target threat requires major field reorganization. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:24](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L24).
- [x] **2.1-I13 · definition:** Background felt tone sets the average level on which fit-baseline rests, influenced by physiology and available capacity. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:26](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L26).
- [x] **2.1-I14 · mechanism:** Held contraction and unsatisfied pulling/pushing targets bottleneck capacity; persistent target gaps differ from fading ordinary surprises. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:26](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L26).
- [x] **2.1-I15 · example:** Ocean level represents background tone, flat surface fit-baseline, and waves cost deviations; identical insight can feel mundane or exciting at different background levels. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:28](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L28).
- [x] **2.1-I16 · mechanism:** Constant near-expected integrations cumulatively embody fit; dependency-tree trunk organizes ordinary input without major surprise and anchors comparisons. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:30](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L30).
- [x] **2.1-I17 · example:** Predictably cheap elevator music becomes mundane baseline; failed gestalt compression costs extra parsing; rich input fitting a known gestalt yields positive deviation. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:30](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L30).
- [x] **2.1-I18 · claim:** Cost deviations occur over milliseconds or minutes and across aesthetic and bodily inputs. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:30](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L30).
- [x] **2.1-I19 · mechanism:** Outer prior changes are cheap; deeper violations force dependent restructuring and larger spikes or fragmentation. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:32](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L32).
- [x] **2.1-I20 · example:** Shame can hurt strongly through deep self/security priors such as I am bad, generating discrepancies even for low-level input. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:32](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L32).
- [x] **2.1-I21 · claim:** Outer branches update most and can cascade inward; deep priors are continuously active, rarely violated, and mostly contribute through steady baseline integration. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:32](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L32).
- [x] **2.1-I22 · definition:** Compression progress is the rate integration cost falls relative to a target, tracking feeling over longer time slices. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:34](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L34).
- [x] **2.1-I23 · caveat:** Too easy, productive struggle, and too hard are practical proxy slopes, not exhaustive or invariably simple forms. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:34](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L34).
- [x] **2.1-I24 · claim:** Too easy leaves little cost to reduce and usually boredom; productive struggle steadily compresses costly uncertain input and can yield curiosity, flow, or aesthetic pleasure. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:34](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L34).
- [x] **2.1-I25 · citation:** Goldilocks Effect describes disengagement from overly predictable or surprising sequences; Eighty-Five Percent Rule is quoted as a training sweet spot for fastest learning, without URLs. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:34](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L34).
- [x] **2.1-I26 · mechanism:** Too-hard input fails to reduce cost or increases it, lacks structured compression and iterated specificity, and accumulates tension, frustration, or overwhelm. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:34](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L34).
- [x] **2.1-I27 · claim:** Difficulty and progress are relative to active targets across time horizons, not intrinsic properties of an activity. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:36](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L36).
- [x] **2.1-I28 · example:** Problem sets may show little local progress yet serve becoming a mathematician; locally productive side branches may fail the larger target. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:36](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L36).
- [x] **2.1-I29 · mechanism:** Simultaneous nested target signals are precision-weighted; aligned targets lend meaning, conflicts produce mixed feelings, and deep weighted targets tend to dominate. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:36](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L36).
- [x] **2.1-I30 · claim:** Felt tone over time signals whether cost falls as quickly as predicted relative to active targets. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:36](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L36).
- [x] **2.1-I31 · caveat:** Model is a phenomenological synthesis, not exact metabolic reporting; it draws on predictive processing, interoception, allostasis, fluency, and aesthetic prediction error. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:38](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L38).
- [x] **2.1-I32 · citation:** Free Energy Principle is described as organizing perception/action around prediction-error reduction; active inference/allostasis inform proactive bodily regulation, without source links. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:38](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L38).
- [x] **2.1-I33 · definition:** Allostasis is proactive bodily resource regulation, anticipating and preparing for needs; interoception senses, interprets, and integrates internal bodily signals consciously and unconsciously. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:40](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L40).
- [x] **2.1-I34 · claim:** Nervous-system state, cortisol, and inflammation modulate background tone through interoception; inflammation is said to produce fatigue, inactivity, withdrawal, and depressed mood. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:40](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L40).
- [x] **2.1-I35 · mechanism:** Stress biases threat attention into feedback loops; a drained/threatened body has less complexity-handling capacity and anticipates more costly input. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:40](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L40).
- [x] **2.1-I36 · mechanism:** Releasing held contraction and reducing target precision frees precommitted capacity and can raise tone; chronic stress and poor health lower it. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:42](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L42).
- [x] **2.1-I37 · claim:** Long-horizon targets are current expectations of progress; tone depends on whether highly weighted targets are achieved, achievable, threatened, or sufficiently handled, not just pleasure. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:44](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L44).
- [x] **2.1-I38 · example:** Difficult work can coexist with deep okay-ness when long-term success is trusted; pleasure can coexist with tension from an unachieved deeper target. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:44](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L44).
- [x] **2.1-I39 · definition:** Handling a target can change the world, the actions intended to reach it, or the target itself. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:44](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L44).
- [x] **2.1-I40 · mechanism:** Target-directed actions are approximate bets; misidentifying error closes a proxy while invariant real gaps persist across new settings. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:46](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L46).
- [x] **2.1-I41 · example:** Leaving a relationship for expected happiness is a bet rather than guaranteed precise closure; recurring dissatisfaction signals an unresolved real gap. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:46](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L46).
- [x] **2.1-I42 · claim:** Beyond physiology, durable tone shifts require persistent target-gap changes; material acquisition adapts into prediction, whereas loosening targets or ending chronic pain removes enduring cost. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:48](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L48).
- [x] **2.1-I43 · claim:** Author lists exercise, sleep, morning light, slow breathing, humming, and dancing as best-studied aids, while suggesting individual possibilities exceed current literature. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:50](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L50).
- [x] **2.1-I44 · caveat:** Meditation research is said to focus on novices and understudy advanced states; experimentation is guided by reducing processing demand or increasing available bodily/psychological capacity. — [sections/the-energetic-triad.md:50](sections/the-energetic-triad.md#L50).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Cost is relative limited processing capacity, not reported calories; predicted versus actual integration cost yields resonance, baseline or friction.
- Easy perception can still threaten high-precision targets; baseline tone and depth of violated priors shape spikes, while ordinary prediction gaps fade differently from persistent target gaps.
- Compression progress is target-relative: boredom, productive struggle and overwhelm depend on nested goals, precision and time horizons rather than difficulty alone.
- Allostasis/interoception, stress and inflammation modulate available capacity; this is a proposed phenomenological synthesis rather than exact metabolic reporting.
- Long-horizon targets can make hard work feel okay or pleasure tense; proxy actions can leave real gaps intact, while durable relief requires persistent gap/precision changes and physiological support.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Cost means relative demand on limited processing capacity rather than experienced calories; the triad is a proposed synthesis, not literal metabolic reporting.
- Cheaper-than-predicted integration correlates with resonance, expected integration with fit-baseline, and more-expensive integration with friction.
- Felt-tone magnitude depends on prior depth, while background tone depends on physiology, contraction, and persistent precision-weighted target gaps.
- Over time, compression progress is assessed relative to several nested targets, producing boredom, productive struggle, or overwhelm rather than one universally easy or hard activity.
- Durable relief depends on changing persistent target gaps; actions can instead close proxies, leaving dissatisfaction recurrent.
