**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Haptic contraction is felt muscular/visceral/vascular tightening stabilizing held priors or targets; expansion releases it, and functional contraction need not be harmful.
- Johnson’s speculative vasocomputation links ambiguous-pattern collapse, memory and latching; deep tension may persist while shallow unlatched tension releases, with blood-flow data explicitly not evidence about priors.
- Somatic priors and interoceptive evidence mutually reinforce field configurations; repeated release with clarity/slack can weaken deeper reinforcement, analogous to learning in reverse.
- Representations act through retuned fields, usually shallowly; Ideal Parent Figure repeated trusted safety/action evidence can affect deeper attachment, unlike repetition alone.
- Embodied intuition guides meaningful notation but notation can scaffold later embodiment; OpenAI/ClosedAI shows conditioning- and target-dependent framing rather than universally positive open/negative closed.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Haptic contraction is the felt somatic form of a held prior; expansion releases it, and functional contraction need not harm well-being.
- The vascular substrate account and links between prior depth and latching are hypotheses, not established measurements of priors.
- Somatic enactment reinforces priors through interoceptive feedback and can bookmark unfinished targets or reinstate remembered configurations.
- Representations usually change superficial unlatched patterns; trusted repeated corrective evidence and action may update deeper attachment and contraction patterns.
- Notation and insight gain meaning through embodied/representational feedback; context-dependent wording can index somatic field configurations.

### 2.3 — Slack

- [x] **2.3-I01 · claim:** Slack was invoked without useful definition in 1.2 and triad; this is the explicit-definition location for earlier usage. — [sections/slack.md:1](sections/slack.md#L1).
- [x] **2.3-I02 · definition:** Slack is capacity not committed to holding current field configuration; practical capacity spans attention, regulation, representations, physiology, and time. — [sections/slack.md:10](sections/slack.md#L10).
- [x] **2.3-I03 · mechanism:** Slack lets competing branches coexist and error remain meaningful rather than immediately resolved by habitual active inference. — [sections/slack.md:10](sections/slack.md#L10).
- [x] **2.3-I04 · caveat:** Slack is a field property, not a depleting fuel tank; ego-depletion account is unreliable and failed replication. — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12).
- [x] **2.3-I05 · caveat:** Slack is not metabolic budget or thinking harder; awake functioning already uses most required brain energy and focused thought adds little. — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12).
- [x] **2.3-I06 · mechanism:** Effort allocation and worthwhile-action judgments change with context rather than simply fuel exhaustion. — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12).
- [x] **2.3-I07 · claim:** Pain and fatigue can make the same action harder and promote avoidance or easier habits. — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12).
- [x] **2.3-I08 · caveat:** Apparent calm can reflect collapsed options, not slack. — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12).
- [x] **2.3-I09 · claim:** Slack is phenomenologically visible only under disturbance, not directly readable from inside the field; recovery time is a contextual proxy. — [sections/slack.md:14](sections/slack.md#L14); [sections/slack.md:34](sections/slack.md#L34).
- [x] **2.3-I10 · definition:** Slack is always for a particular demand; intellectual uncertainty versus social rejection illustrates nontransferable capacity. — [sections/slack.md:16](sections/slack.md#L16).
- [x] **2.3-I11 · citation:** Physiological reserve is the basal-to-maximum-response gap, analogizing extra demand tolerated before normal recovery fails. — [sections/slack.md:16](sections/slack.md#L16).
- [x] **2.3-I12 · mechanism:** Trusted environments lower configuration-holding cost; contracted latch permanently commits capacity, so release returns room rather than adding content. — [sections/slack.md:16](sections/slack.md#L16).
- [x] **2.3-I13 · mechanism:** Predictable routines/rituals reduce surprise and spare-capacity requirements; volatile contexts require more slack for the same updating. — [sections/slack.md:18](sections/slack.md#L18).
- [x] **2.3-I14 · mechanism:** Little slack defaults to deep habits/priors and increased model precision rather than holding error or exploring alternatives. — [sections/slack.md:20](sections/slack.md#L20).
- [x] **2.3-I15 · claim:** Low slack simplifies before fragmenting: distinctions least relevant to dominant target collapse first; expansion requires slack, matching 1.3 many-to-one asymmetry. — [sections/slack.md:20](sections/slack.md#L20).
- [x] **2.3-I16 · caveat:** Low slack narrows available interpretations and actions, not all activity; threat can accelerate already-oriented updating while alternatives become unreachable. — [sections/slack.md:20](sections/slack.md#L20).
- [x] **2.3-I17 · citation:** Scarcity tunnels attention onto urgent wanted-state gaps and excludes longer-range targets from action though underlying structures persist. — [sections/slack.md:20](sections/slack.md#L20).
- [x] **2.3-I18 · claim:** Slack is not one scalar: sleep loss may impair attention while sparing reasoning; stress may impair flexibility while improving inhibition. — [sections/slack.md:22](sections/slack.md#L22).
- [x] **2.3-I19 · definition:** Profile includes physiology, attention/working-memory, expected controllability, alternative configurations, environmental/social support, and consolidation time. — [sections/slack.md:22](sections/slack.md#L22).
- [x] **2.3-I20 · mechanism:** Dimensions can substitute (notation for working memory) and serve different change phases, such as initial attention versus later safety/time stabilization. — [sections/slack.md:22](sections/slack.md#L22).
- [x] **2.3-I21 · caveat:** Existing capacity helps only when reachable from the needed state; stress/anxiety can render work-state intelligence and flexibility inaccessible. — [sections/slack.md:24](sections/slack.md#L24).
- [x] **2.3-I22 · example:** Diverse recovery routes are more robust than copies: three breathing methods may share anxiety failure, unlike the source’s diverse paths "breaking, walking, and social contact" utilizing different systems. — [sections/slack.md:26](sections/slack.md#L26).
- [x] **2.3-I23 · mechanism:** Spare openness can be spent immediately on renewed prediction/collapse; equanimity holds multiple possibilities without premature resolution so slack remains usable. — [sections/slack.md:28](sections/slack.md#L28).
- [x] **2.3-I24 · definition:** Proposed disturbance knocks system out of current attractor into another; basin width/depth scales required perturbation. — [sections/slack.md:30](sections/slack.md#L30).
- [x] **2.3-I25 · definition:** Capture means remaining in the new attractor/basin rather than returning to the old; two proxy questions are susceptibility to capture and recovery speed. — [sections/slack.md:30](sections/slack.md#L30).
- [x] **2.3-I26 · example:** Transient stress followed by calm is not capture; extended stress as default is; recovery returns to old or higher-order attractor. — [sections/slack.md:32](sections/slack.md#L32).
- [x] **2.3-I27 · definition:** Higher-order attractor integrates a wider range of competing input without narrower collapse. — [sections/slack.md:32](sections/slack.md#L32).
- [x] **2.3-I28 · claim:** Recovery time is an imperfect proxy scaled to disturbance, whose magnitude depends on how deep priors are shaken, not inherent input strength. — [sections/slack.md:34](sections/slack.md#L34).
- [x] **2.3-I29 · definition:** Shaken priors mean destabilizing prediction error induces clamping to singular interpretations/actions. — [sections/slack.md:34](sections/slack.md#L34).
- [x] **2.3-I30 · example:** Changed daily plans usually demand less slack than a crash destabilizing felt safety; prolonged small-event recovery can indicate low affected-channel slack. — [sections/slack.md:34](sections/slack.md#L34).
- [x] **2.3-I31 · caveat:** High cognitive flexibility/time slack can coexist with chronic-illness physiological vulnerability, giving physiological inputs greater disturbance magnitude. — [sections/slack.md:34](sections/slack.md#L34).
- [x] **2.3-I32 · caveat:** Slow recovery can adapt to harsh conditions; problematic recovery goes toward lower-order deep-basin states that cut off experience and reduce slack. — [sections/slack.md:36](sections/slack.md#L36).
- [x] **2.3-I33 · citation:** fuel tank that simply depletes with use — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12). Sources: [https://doi.org/10.1037/xge0000083](https://doi.org/10.1037/xge0000083).
- [x] **2.3-I34 · citation:** "Mental energy runs out" (ego depletion) — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12). Sources: [https://doi.org/10.1177/1745691616652873](https://doi.org/10.1177/1745691616652873).
- [x] **2.3-I35 · citation:** Your brain is already consuming most of its required energy simply by being awake and functioning, so focused thought only adds a small amount of cost — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4387513/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4387513/).
- [x] **2.3-I36 · citation:** When mental effort feels too costly to the system, the issue is that the brain decides effort is not worth allocating within the given context — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3856320/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3856320/).
- [x] **2.3-I37 · citation:** When the system seems to run "out of fuel", the brain has changed how worthwhile an action feels — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3718823/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3718823/).
- [x] **2.3-I38 · citation:** pain — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12). Sources: [https://elifesciences.org/articles/59410](https://elifesciences.org/articles/59410).
- [x] **2.3-I39 · citation:** fatigue — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12). Sources: [https://doi.org/10.1016/j.psychsport.2017.08.013](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.psychsport.2017.08.013).
- [x] **2.3-I40 · citation:** a person can appear calm because they have collapsed their options — [sections/slack.md:12](sections/slack.md#L12). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4495877/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC4495877/).
- [x] **2.3-I41 · citation:** difference between the basal level of a system and its maximal capacity to respond — [sections/slack.md:16](sections/slack.md#L16). Sources: [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5861884/](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5861884/).
- [x] **2.3-I42 · citation:** Under scarcity, attention "tunnels" onto the most urgent gap in wanted states — [sections/slack.md:20](sections/slack.md#L20). Sources: [https://www.hks.harvard.edu/centers/cid/publications/books/scarcity-why-having-too-little-means-so-much](https://www.hks.harvard.edu/centers/cid/publications/books/scarcity-why-having-too-little-means-so-much).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Slack is relational uncommitted field capacity, not fuel, effort or calmness; it permits holding competing interpretations and is observable under disturbance.
- Environment, routine and released latches reduce commitment costs; low slack simplifies/narrows experience and tunnels targets without destroying stored structure or all oriented updating.
- Slack is a substitutable multidimensional profile bounded by reachability; diverse regulation paths resist correlated failure better than multiple copies of one path.
- Equanimity keeps spare openness from immediately being spent on resolving uncertainty.
- Capture and recovery time relative to disturbance depth are practical proxies, not exact measurements; slow recovery can be adaptive unless it settles into restrictive lower-order basins.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Slack is context-relative uncommitted practical processing capacity, not a universal fuel tank, calmness, or a literal energy scalar.
- Low slack favors habitual collapse, fewer distinctions and narrower reachable action rather than simply stopping all updates.
- Slack is a multidimensional accessible profile; environment, routines, external notation, diverse pathways, and equanimity can change usable availability.
- Capture and disturbance-scaled recovery time provide proposed proxies, interpreted relative to affected priors and capacity channels.
- Slow recovery may be adaptive; persistent narrowing into lower-order deep-basin states is the problematic case.

### 2.4 — Language

- [x] **2.4-I01 · definition:** Language transfers information as a shared compression channel binding nodes across embodied, representational, and notational layers. — [sections/language.md:1](sections/language.md#L1).
- [x] **2.4-I02 · example:** Unicorn evokes images, memories, and bodily feelings despite physical absence; language points beyond present objects. — [sections/language.md:1](sections/language.md#L1).
- [x] **2.4-I03 · mechanism:** Tokens compress many possibilities; receiver reconstructs layered gestalt through their own bow tie/field, constraining interpretation at both ends. — [sections/language.md:9](sections/language.md#L9).
- [x] **2.4-I04 · caveat:** Producer negative valence flavors language and receiver may not recognize state-dependent coloring. — [sections/language.md:9](sections/language.md#L9).
- [x] **2.4-I05 · claim:** Written language is maximally compressed notation; body language, prosody, and expressions are more embodied; author proposes compressed language is more specific/easier to interpret accurately. — [sections/language.md:11](sections/language.md#L11).
- [x] **2.4-I06 · citation:** Tokens elicit right felt senses; writing that feels off exemplifies this felt-sense account. — [sections/language.md:11](sections/language.md#L11).
- [x] **2.4-I07 · mechanism:** Author predicts off-feeling writing reflects greater actual than predicted integration cost, friction, and deletion actions in the dependency tree. — [sections/language.md:11](sections/language.md#L11).
- [x] **2.4-I08 · claim:** Recursive composition combines tokens into new connected pointers; fast automatic language often activates embodied nodes. — [sections/language.md:13](sections/language.md#L13).
- [x] **2.4-I09 · definition:** Shared decompression key is common priors and gestalts letting compressed pointers reconstruct appropriate felt senses, representations, and notation. — [sections/language.md:13](sections/language.md#L13).
- [x] **2.4-I10 · mechanism:** Shared key can regenerate similar feelings across people; mismatched upstream priors reconstruct specificity unlike producer meaning. — [sections/language.md:13](sections/language.md#L13).
- [x] **2.4-I11 · definition:** Language differs from other input by communicative linking of producer output bow tie and receiver decompression bow tie. — [sections/language.md:15](sections/language.md#L15); [sections/language.md:9](sections/language.md#L9).
- [x] **2.4-I12 · claim:** Decompression uses shared invariants; keys diverge with specificity, expertise, and longer texts, making books/poems harder than common words. — [sections/language.md:15](sections/language.md#L15).
- [x] **2.4-I13 · caveat:** Language does not determine fields: it is one input among bodily/environmental influences, though strong biases can feel deterministic. — [sections/language.md:17](sections/language.md#L17).
- [x] **2.4-I14 · mechanism:** Language biases salience and right-side possibilities through compression shaped by existing memory and structure. — [sections/language.md:17](sections/language.md#L17).
- [x] **2.4-I15 · citation:** Associative token networks such as cat/animal bundle neighboring properties, including valence. — [sections/language.md:17](sections/language.md#L17).
- [x] **2.4-I16 · example:** Researching depression can encounter suicide associations that bundle felt properties and influence configuration; this is a proposed possibility. — [sections/language.md:17](sections/language.md#L17).
- [x] **2.4-I17 · mechanism:** Repeated token association further couples knowledge-node networks and biases priors; metaphors instantiate associated compression/decompression properties. — [sections/language.md:17](sections/language.md#L17).
- [x] **2.4-I18 · example:** Proust madeleine analogy shows one input generating mutually reinforcing memories/feelings like linguistic node reconstruction. — [sections/language.md:19](sections/language.md#L19).

- [x] **2.4-I21 · example:** Actors adopting character stances can improvise matching dialogue; imitation cannot exactly reproduce another writer’s rich low-level tension landscape. — [sections/language.md:25](sections/language.md#L25).

### 3.1 — Metaphors

- [x] **3.1-I01 · definition:** Metaphor is a translation mechanism within the representational layer that makes abstract things meaningful to the mind-body system. — [sections/metaphors.md:9](sections/metaphors.md#L9); [sections/metaphors.md:15](sections/metaphors.md#L15).
- [x] **3.1-I02 · claim:** The author attributes to philosophers the view that early language grounded abstractions in physical surroundings rather than beginning literally and adding metaphor later. — [sections/metaphors.md:9](sections/metaphors.md#L9).
- [x] **3.1-I03 · citation:** Lakoff and Johnson, Metaphors We Live By: ordinary conceptual systems are fundamentally metaphorical, influencing thought and action as well as language. — [sections/metaphors.md:11](sections/metaphors.md#L11).
- [x] **3.1-I04 · mechanism:** Unconscious metaphoric structures shape perception, interpersonal interaction, decisions, and action because language follows how things are conceived. — [sections/metaphors.md:11](sections/metaphors.md#L11); [sections/metaphors.md:13](sections/metaphors.md#L13).
- [x] **3.1-I05 · example:** Argument-as-war recruits winning and defending; a societal argument-as-dance framing might instead support balanced, aesthetically pleasing interaction. — [sections/metaphors.md:13](sections/metaphors.md#L13).
- [x] **3.1-I06 · claim:** Representational metaphor shapes the notational and embodied layers through language and behavior. — [sections/metaphors.md:15](sections/metaphors.md#L15).
- [x] **3.1-I07 · example:** Argument-as-war draws on felt bracing and charging; time-as-money draws on embodied scarcity and resource depletion. — [sections/metaphors.md:15](sections/metaphors.md#L15).
- [x] **3.1-I08 · definition:** Johnson’s The Body in the Mind supplies image schema: recurring bodily patterns such as containment, balance, or paths, formed through physical experience before words. — [sections/metaphors.md:15](sections/metaphors.md#L15).
- [x] **3.1-I09 · mechanism:** A felt sense lacking its own internal representation can become explicit via metaphor, with embodied experience supplying crucial feedback. — [sections/metaphors.md:15](sections/metaphors.md#L15).
- [x] **3.1-I10 · mechanism:** Bottom-up translation proceeds from embodied felt sense through mapping onto an abstract target to representation, language, and enacted behavior. — [sections/metaphors.md:15](sections/metaphors.md#L15).
- [x] **3.1-I11 · mechanism:** Processing notation can run back down through war imagery to activate associated bodily characteristics; an ungrounded metaphor would be hollow. — [sections/metaphors.md:15](sections/metaphors.md#L15).
- [x] **3.1-I12 · example:** Feeling down and being on top of the world ground affect in gravity, upright strength, and collapsing disempowerment. — [sections/metaphors.md:15](sections/metaphors.md#L15).
- [x] **3.1-I13 · claim:** Because mathematical objects are often not directly sensory, understanding often uses metaphorical embodied grounding and spatial or physical language. — [sections/metaphors.md:17](sections/metaphors.md#L17).
- [x] **3.1-I14 · citation:** Thurston describes intuition, association, and metaphor as important mathematical faculties; quieting and focusing helps build their connections while words, logic, and detailed pictures can inhibit them. — [sections/metaphors.md:17](sections/metaphors.md#L17).
- [x] **3.1-I15 · claim:** Notation is described as weakest in richness because it cannot capture the full felt sense of an internal representation. — [sections/metaphors.md:17](sections/metaphors.md#L17).
- [x] **3.1-I16 · caveat:** Representation is broader than metaphor: understanding constructs a representation fitting the field, including bodily metaphor, images, or mental simulation. — [sections/metaphors.md:19](sections/metaphors.md#L19).
- [x] **3.1-I17 · caveat:** Draft summary is an author placeholder, protected verbatim; no summary content may be invented. — [sections/metaphors.md:1](sections/metaphors.md#L1).

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Metaphor is representational translation grounded in embodied patterns, not merely poetic language; Lakoff/Johnson connect it to thought and action.
- War versus dance frames reorganize arguments/actions, while image schemas and scarcity/gravity give time-is-money and up/down metaphors their grounding.
- The stack operates both upward from felt sense to model/notation and downward to enactment.
- Thurston’s mathematical report emphasizes intuition/association and quieting verbal noise; metaphor is one of several runnable representations fitting a field.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Metaphor makes abstractions meaningful by mapping bodily structure into representations that shape language and action.
- Metaphors are conceptual structures, not merely poetic decorations; war and dance framings can change disagreement behavior.
- Embodied image schemas ground metaphor, and notation can reactivate the bodily structure in the reverse direction.
- Mathematical understanding uses intuition, association, and metaphor beyond what notation can capture.
- Metaphor is only one representational method; images and mental simulations can also fit the field.

### 3.2 — Intuition

- [x] **3.2-I01 · definition:** Intuition is implicit priors speaking directly: the field generates a salient representation or feeling before explicit derivation, with precision making it worth attention. — [sections/intuition.md:1](sections/intuition.md#L1).
- [x] **3.2-I02 · caveat:** Intuition is fast and pre-verbal, landing in the representational or embodied layer rather than notation; it is not experienced as algorithmic. — [sections/intuition.md:1](sections/intuition.md#L1).
- [x] **3.2-I03 · definition:** A fit signal reports satisfaction of the current field; intuition instead denotes rapid salient generation, whose output can reveal experiential information through externalization. — [sections/intuition.md:1](sections/intuition.md#L1).
- [x] **3.2-I04 · mechanism:** Positive felt-tone differences draw intuition toward possibilities; negative differences pull back, including an unexplained off feeling as pre-verbal assessment. — [sections/intuition.md:10](sections/intuition.md#L10); [sections/intuition.md:12](sections/intuition.md#L12).
- [x] **3.2-I05 · example:** Patrick House’s military-driver story depicts abrupt retreat from an Iraqi road on a bad feeling before an explanation was available. — [sections/intuition.md:12](sections/intuition.md#L12).
- [x] **3.2-I06 · caveat:** The author proposes that unusual silence may have violated the trained field; later notation suggested mothers kept children inside because they expected danger. This causal reading is tentative. — [sections/intuition.md:12](sections/intuition.md#L12).
- [x] **3.2-I07 · mechanism:** An intuitive embodied assessment can precede notation naming the violated properties; background field processing may also generate aha moments on other timescales. — [sections/intuition.md:12](sections/intuition.md#L12).
- [x] **3.2-I08 · mechanism:** After intuition surfaces, optional explicit work names satisfied or violated field properties, either easily or through slow derivation. — [sections/intuition.md:14](sections/intuition.md#L14).
- [x] **3.2-I09 · caveat:** Compressing intuition into notation loses some content; articulability measures compressibility, not whether the experience is intuition. — [sections/intuition.md:14](sections/intuition.md#L14).
- [x] **3.2-I10 · claim:** Without appropriate notational knowledge, naming an intuition is harder because the required expressive forms are unavailable. — [sections/intuition.md:14](sections/intuition.md#L14).
- [x] **3.2-I11 · claim:** Novice intuition rapidly outputs default priors poorly tuned to context; expert intuition outputs a contextually tuned field, with tacit expertise its conditioned actionable expression. — [sections/intuition.md:16](sections/intuition.md#L16).
- [x] **3.2-I12 · citation:** Bessis describes mathematical intuition as everyday intuition developed and solidified by confrontation with language and logic. — [sections/intuition.md:16](sections/intuition.md#L16).
- [x] **3.2-I13 · definition:** The author names that confrontation the loop of refinement; intuition is only as trustworthy as its originating field. — [sections/intuition.md:16](sections/intuition.md#L16).
- [x] **3.2-I14 · caveat:** Without refinement, a miscalibrated intuition can feel identical to a calibrated one and be confidently mistaken for correctness. — [sections/intuition.md:16](sections/intuition.md#L16).
- [x] **3.2-I15 · claim:** Mathematics exposes refinement by translating pre-verbal intuition into notation and testing through formal proof. — [sections/intuition.md:18](sections/intuition.md#L18).
- [x] **3.2-I16 · caveat:** Draft summary is an author placeholder, protected verbatim; no summary content may be invented. — [sections/intuition.md:1](sections/intuition.md#L1).

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Intuition rapidly generates salient pre-verbal embodied/representational output and is not itself a fit signal, though it reaches/recoils through felt differences.
- House’s Iraq-road driver story illustrates trained implicit violation detection preceding explicit explanation.
- Naming intuition is optional lossy compression; articulability measures notational compressibility rather than intuition status.
- Novice and expert intuitions can feel alike; reliability depends on calibrated fields via confrontation with language/logic and formal testing.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Intuition is a rapid, salient, pre-verbal field output in representation or embodiment, not itself the fit signal.
- Positive and negative felt-tone differences can guide intuitive approach or withdrawal before an explanation exists.
- Explicit articulation is optional and lossy; difficulty naming intuition concerns compressibility rather than its status as intuition.
- Expert intuition differs from novice intuition through field calibration, not through how certainty feels.
- Refinement through language, logic, and proof is needed because miscalibrated intuition can feel as convincing as calibrated intuition.

### 3.3 — Mathematics

- [x] **3.3-I01 · caveat:** Mathematicians’ subjective reports illuminate layer traversal but reporting tacit knowledge is itself lossy. — [sections/mathematics.md:9](sections/mathematics.md#L9).
- [x] **3.3-I02 · citation:** Einstein’s response to Hadamard describes voluntarily reproducible and combinable signs and images, rather than written or spoken words, as thought elements. — [sections/mathematics.md:11](sections/mathematics.md#L11).
- [x] **3.3-I03 · citation:** Einstein calls combinatory play essential to productive thought before communicable logical construction. — [sections/mathematics.md:11](sections/mathematics.md#L11).
- [x] **3.3-I04 · citation:** Einstein identifies visual and muscular thought elements and describes conventional words and signs as laboriously sought only at a secondary stage. — [sections/mathematics.md:11](sections/mathematics.md#L11).
- [x] **3.3-I05 · citation:** Hadamard’s survey asks what mental images and internal words mathematicians use, including motor, auditory, visual, and mixed modalities. — [sections/mathematics.md:11](sections/mathematics.md#L11).
- [x] **3.3-I06 · caveat:** Taken together these reports suggest at least some epistemic invention is largely pre-verbal, with notation and language later—not a universal claim about all thought. — [sections/mathematics.md:11](sections/mathematics.md#L11).
- [x] **3.3-I07 · citation:** Grothendieck’s Récoltes et Semailles describes mental images as the driving soul of his work and says even simple mathematical text must evoke experienced meaning, images, and intuition. — [sections/mathematics.md:13](sections/mathematics.md#L13).
- [x] **3.3-I08 · citation:** Grothendieck describes interrogating things by examining, solidifying, clarifying, and detailing mental images, as in recalling a dream. — [sections/mathematics.md:13](sections/mathematics.md#L13).
- [x] **3.3-I09 · citation:** Grothendieck describes difficulty following others without translating their thoughts into his own images and style. — [sections/mathematics.md:13](sections/mathematics.md#L13).
- [x] **3.3-I10 · mechanism:** The author interprets Einstein and Grothendieck as manipulating representation more directly than notation; formula changes express and update representational knowledge bidirectionally. — [sections/mathematics.md:13](sections/mathematics.md#L13).
- [x] **3.3-I11 · citation:** Tao distinguishes close-collaborator communication from colleague communication: knowing the collaborator’s mental model enables intuitive exchanges close to mathematical thinking. — [sections/mathematics.md:15](sections/mathematics.md#L15).
- [x] **3.3-I12 · mechanism:** A shared vivid representation permits compressed gestures because the receiver reconstructs unstated structure from a model already carried. — [sections/mathematics.md:15](sections/mathematics.md#L15).
- [x] **3.3-I13 · caveat:** Without shared representation, more structure must be explicit in still-lossy notation and the listener reconstructs a model; shared context reduces rebuilding rather than eliminating notation. — [sections/mathematics.md:15](sections/mathematics.md#L15).
- [x] **3.3-I14 · citation:** Bessis’s Mathematica claims mathematical power lies in intuition rather than logic; proofs are downstream products of cultivating inner intuition and imagery. — [sections/mathematics.md:17](sections/mathematics.md#L17).
- [x] **3.3-I15 · citation:** Bessis describes secret math as mental representations and abstract often-visual sensations obvious and pleasurable to mathematicians, yet difficult to share. — [sections/mathematics.md:17](sections/mathematics.md#L17).
- [x] **3.3-I16 · example:** Bessis compares inventing mathematical language and symbols to musicians inventing notation to transcribe compositions. — [sections/mathematics.md:17](sections/mathematics.md#L17).
- [x] **3.3-I17 · claim:** Compression into legible notation is pragmatically necessary for collaboration and preserving progress despite the centrality of lower layers. — [sections/mathematics.md:17](sections/mathematics.md#L17).
- [x] **3.3-I18 · citation:** Bessis calls mathematicians’ intuition their masterpiece and lifetime accomplishment; the author reads trained lower-layer intuition as the deeper achievement. — [sections/mathematics.md:17](sections/mathematics.md#L17).
- [x] **3.3-I19 · claim:** The author infers that mathematical difficulty includes knowing when and how representation corresponds to truth, not notation alone. — [sections/mathematics.md:19](sections/mathematics.md#L19).
- [x] **3.3-I20 · caveat:** These reports supply neither a complete formal image-generation rule nor a complete image-evaluation system; pre-formal checking often starts with an internal directional sense. — [sections/mathematics.md:19](sections/mathematics.md#L19).
- [x] **3.3-I21 · mechanism:** Proof becomes possible when intuition is compressed into notation, and notational feedback can revise intuition. — [sections/mathematics.md:19](sections/mathematics.md#L19).
