**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- An emotion is a context-sensitive whole field gestalt, while feeling is its inside presentation and tone only valence; mood is slower contextual bias.
- Conceptual chunks compress variable fear instances with fuzzy thresholds, and multiple targets/policies depend on precision/context rather than one fixed action.
- Bodily interoception/readiness participates in constructing emotion; shared deep architecture coexists with culturally malleable categories and learned granularity.
- Somatic feedback stabilizes emotional attractors without making all grief rigid; mixed emotions need one multi-target field and expression cannot reliably reveal it from one cue.
- Regulation is part of the field; reappraisal changes organization, whereas suppression blocks expression while held somatic organization persists.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Emotions are embodied, context-sensitive field gestalts, distinct from feelings, valence, and slower moods.
- Precision, learning, fuzzy compression, bodily constraints, and culture jointly organize emotional episodes rather than merely labeling signals.
- Emotions relate multiple targets and make multiple actions available; mixed emotions fit within one field and expression cannot uniquely reveal it.
- Somatic feedback stabilizes habitual emotions, while regulation alters configuration; suppression can preserve and rigidify it.

### 5.2 — Field Dissonance

- [x] **5.2-I01 · definition:** Field dissonance is conflicting active properties/policies while organization or action must settle; sources include priors, body, representations, social constraints, and targets, beyond explicit beliefs. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:1](sections/field-dissonance.md#L1).
- [x] **5.2-I02 · example:** Smiling and saying fine can satisfy social constraints while protective withdrawal loses; mutually exclusive policies resolve toward higher weighting without resolving violated properties. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:10](sections/field-dissonance.md#L10).
- [x] **5.2-I03 · mechanism:** A cached dominant policy can leave losing properties active in posture, tone, contraction, or thoughts; lying may leak through forced laughter and avoided eye contact. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:12](sections/field-dissonance.md#L12).
- [x] **5.2-I04 · caveat:** Unresolved target error need not produce friction or changed tone; friction arises from greater-than-predicted integration difficulty, although negative tone is common. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:12](sections/field-dissonance.md#L12).
- [x] **5.2-I05 · mechanism:** Precision weighting contributes to pre-action control; simultaneous satisfaction and violation explains feeling right and wrong as preferred states compete for limited action channels. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:14](sections/field-dissonance.md#L14).
- [x] **5.2-I06 · caveat:** Behavior cannot uniquely identify a hierarchy/context/time-dependent dissonance configuration; short-lived conflict can preserve alternatives and reduce later suffering. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:16](sections/field-dissonance.md#L16).
- [x] **5.2-I07 · mechanism:** Repeated selection without updating competing structures, or heavily weighted errors blocking stable defaults, creates self-reinforcing attractors; social smiling leaves bodily threat unsatisfied. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:16](sections/field-dissonance.md#L16).
- [x] **5.2-I08 · mechanism:** Resolution paths differ: weighting overpowers, suppression retains excluded material, reframing updates the model, freezing stops action, sequencing smiles now and cries later. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:18](sections/field-dissonance.md#L18).
- [x] **5.2-I09 · definition:** Integration is a structure-preserving transformation generating a new action gestalt that preserves load-bearing target properties and practical constraints rather than choosing existing policies. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:20](sections/field-dissonance.md#L20).
- [x] **5.2-I10 · example:** Safety fantasy can become an enactable policy; a proof student’s visual geometry integrates logical axioms and spatial needs instead of memorization or guessing. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:20](sections/field-dissonance.md#L20).
- [x] **5.2-I11 · definition:** Local targets organize narrow horizons and global targets wider time/context, continuously rather than as a binary; gym versus present comfort illustrates divergence. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:22](sections/field-dissonance.md#L22).
- [x] **5.2-I12 · claim:** Global target is a temporal label for repeated reinstantiation, not continuously present property; apparent irrationality can be immediate comfort gaining motivational precision. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:24](sections/field-dissonance.md#L24).
- [x] **5.2-I13 · definition:** Self-control maintains a weighted global target; discipline reliably reinstantiates it under competitors while preserving revision when local information or conditions warrant. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:26](sections/field-dissonance.md#L26).
- [x] **5.2-I14 · mechanism:** Environment and routines increase intended attractor reachability/minimize friction while blocking short-horizon defaults; global goals should not always dominate. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:26](sections/field-dissonance.md#L26).
- [x] **5.2-I15 · example:** Conflicts are asymmetrical when consciously weighted calm competes with a wider/deeper anxiety basin that reinstates protective contraction more easily. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:28](sections/field-dissonance.md#L28).
- [x] **5.2-I16 · caveat:** Dissonance is not suffering: the loop begins when an active demand becomes highly weighted need for different present experience with low reachability. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:30](sections/field-dissonance.md#L30).
- [x] **5.2-I17 · caveat:** Protected frontmatter scaffolding: unfinished summary placeholder and draft marker must remain exactly; do not supply missing summary. — [sections/field-dissonance.md:1](sections/field-dissonance.md#L1).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Field dissonance is active conflict among embodied/social/representational properties and policies, broader than explicit-belief dissonance; overt compressed action may leave losing demands alive and leaking.
- Precision determines control, and unresolved target error need not produce unexpected-cost friction; short conflicts may aid alternatives whereas repeated unupdated conflict can form attractors.
- Resolution can weight, suppress, reframe, freeze or sequence, or create an integrative SPT action preserving target properties, as a geometric proof model does.
- Local/global targets differ by horizon and re-instantiation, not two fields; discipline sustains reachable wider goals yet remains willing to revise them.
- Target strength differs from basin accessibility, and suffering specifically adds a high-weight demand for different presently hard-to-reach experience.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Field dissonance is simultaneous conflict among active properties or policies, broader than explicit cognitive belief conflict and distinct from suffering.
- A weighted behavioral winner leaves other targets active; leakage or target error need not imply friction, and transient conflict may be useful.
- Persistent unupdated conflict forms attractors, but reframing, sequencing, freezing, suppression, or structure-preserving integration offer distinct resolutions.
- Global goals are repeated longer-horizon target reinstantiations, and disciplined reachability must coexist with revisability rather than rigid domination.

### 5.3 — The Suffering Loop

- [x] **5.3-I01 · claim:** The synthesis distinguishes three phases: first-order hurt, suffering configuration, and maintenance. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:1](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L1).
- [x] **5.3-I02 · definition:** Suffering is negative felt state spreading because a high-weight target demands changed or permanent experience; persistence reflects no easily reachable action, model/target update, or nonresistance. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:10](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L10).
- [x] **5.3-I03 · definition:** Target error is current-target difference; action policies are possible paths to remove it. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:10](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L10).
- [x] **5.3-I04 · caveat:** Technical suffering includes ordinary wanting, while practical usage involves field reorganization and time; chair restlessness versus pain illustrates the distinction; cessation alone is claimed absolutely suffering-free. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:10](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L10).
- [x] **5.3-I05 · claim:** Practical aim is reducing magnitude and widening reachable configurations, not total elimination. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:10](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L10).
- [x] **5.3-I06 · example:** Predicted integration cost 20 versus actual 30 metaphorically produces friction and typically unpleasant tone; fit distance measures input fit. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:12](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L12).
- [x] **5.3-I07 · definition:** First-order hurt is immediate negative aspect before a target demands difference, from threats, violated expectations, loss, overload, or integration mismatch; Buddhist first arrow is unavoidable pain. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:14](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L14). Sources: [https://suttacentral.net/sn36.6/en/sujato?lang=en&layout=plain&reference=none&notes=asterisk&highlight=false&script=latin](https://suttacentral.net/sn36.6/en/sujato?lang=en&layout=plain&reference=none&notes=asterisk&highlight=false&script=latin).
- [x] **5.3-I08 · caveat:** Unsurprising pain, exhaustion, loss, and overload can hurt without becoming suffering, which requires a high-precision change target. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:16](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L16). Sources: [https://suttacentral.net/define/ta%E1%B9%87h%C4%81?lang=en](https://suttacentral.net/define/ta%E1%B9%87h%C4%81?lang=en); [https://neuroticgradientdescent.blogspot.com/2020/01/mistranslating-buddha.html](https://neuroticgradientdescent.blogspot.com/2020/01/mistranslating-buddha.html); [https://suttacentral.net/sn56.11/en/bodhi?lang=en&reference=none&highlight=false](https://suttacentral.net/sn56.11/en/bodhi?lang=en&reference=none&highlight=false).
- [x] **5.3-I09 · definition:** Taṇhā literally thirst/conventionally craving, with contested fused/welded interpretation, is grabby/pushy conditioning that precedes and generates suffering, not suffering itself. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:16](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L16). Sources: [https://suttacentral.net/define/ta%E1%B9%87h%C4%81?lang=en](https://suttacentral.net/define/ta%E1%B9%87h%C4%81?lang=en); [https://neuroticgradientdescent.blogspot.com/2020/01/mistranslating-buddha.html](https://neuroticgradientdescent.blogspot.com/2020/01/mistranslating-buddha.html); [https://suttacentral.net/sn56.11/en/bodhi?lang=en&reference=none&highlight=false](https://suttacentral.net/sn56.11/en/bodhi?lang=en&reference=none&highlight=false).
- [x] **5.3-I10 · citation:** Four Noble Truths identify taṇhā as principal condition for dukkha; retain the exact origin-of-suffering quotation and its linked support. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:16](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L16). Sources: [https://suttacentral.net/define/ta%E1%B9%87h%C4%81?lang=en](https://suttacentral.net/define/ta%E1%B9%87h%C4%81?lang=en); [https://neuroticgradientdescent.blogspot.com/2020/01/mistranslating-buddha.html](https://neuroticgradientdescent.blogspot.com/2020/01/mistranslating-buddha.html); [https://suttacentral.net/sn56.11/en/bodhi?lang=en&reference=none&highlight=false](https://suttacentral.net/sn56.11/en/bodhi?lang=en&reference=none&highlight=false).
- [x] **5.3-I11 · definition:** Second arrow is reactive second-order stress: aversion evades present state, craving obtains/maintains target; precision organizes error closure and reachability affects duration/intensity. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:18](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L18). Sources: [https://suttacentral.net/sn36.6/en/sujato?lang=en&layout=plain&reference=none&notes=asterisk&highlight=false&script=latin](https://suttacentral.net/sn36.6/en/sujato?lang=en&layout=plain&reference=none&notes=asterisk&highlight=false&script=latin).
- [x] **5.3-I12 · example:** Nearby cake has reachable pleasure target/low magnitude; grief’s return-to-life target or emotional denial is described as impossible and magnifies suffering. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:18](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L18). Sources: [https://suttacentral.net/sn36.6/en/sujato?lang=en&layout=plain&reference=none&notes=asterisk&highlight=false&script=latin](https://suttacentral.net/sn36.6/en/sujato?lang=en&layout=plain&reference=none&notes=asterisk&highlight=false&script=latin).
- [x] **5.3-I13 · claim:** Aversion combines hurt with rejection target; craving combines desired-state absence with must-have target, and absence can itself be felt lack/hurt. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:22](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L22); [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:25](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L25).
- [x] **5.3-I14 · mechanism:** Craving and aversion co-arise as preferred-versus-actual discrepancy, like up/down; they need not feel alike or both consciously appear. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:27](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L27).
- [x] **5.3-I15 · mechanism:** Maintenance active inference can change world/body, model, or target: leave hurtful situation, contract/suppress, reinterpret, or expand/allow to reduce demand precision. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:29](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L29).
- [x] **5.3-I16 · claim:** Slack holds error open and competing dependency branches available; without it hurt automatically becomes suffering (taṇhā becomes destiny). — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:31](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L31); [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:39](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L39).
- [x] **5.3-I17 · mechanism:** Recurring muscular, visceral, vascular, autonomic patterns stabilize priors and prepare actions; violated predictions cause interoceptive friction and defensive tightening. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:33](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L33).
- [x] **5.3-I18 · definition:** Maintenance recursively couples hurt and taṇhā: sustained haptic contraction enacts and weights target, produces unpleasant evidence, and confirms further control demand. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:35](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L35).
- [x] **5.3-I19 · caveat:** Haptic contraction is one somatic expression, not identical to craving/aversion, which name direction of control effort. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:35](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L35).
- [x] **5.3-I20 · mechanism:** Highly weighted unreachable targets rigidify repeated failed control; explicit chain links unpleasantness, must-not, stopping action, contraction, new evidence, intolerance, and stronger stopping. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:37](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L37); [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:39](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L39).
- [x] **5.3-I21 · example:** Repetition forms suffering attractor; rumination reconstructs memories presently and reinstates original configuration and bodily response. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:41](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L41).
- [x] **5.3-I22 · mechanism:** Repeated contraction can latch without ongoing effort/original trigger; mundane conditions reinstate habitual suffering, and suppression through tensing causes this damage. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:43](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L43).
- [x] **5.3-I23 · mechanism:** Epistemic contraction defends models against disconfirming friction by suppressing input/denying ignorance; deep dependencies raise revision cost and defensive entrenchment. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:45](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L45).
- [x] **5.3-I24 · caveat:** Phenomenological and epistemic ignorance share treating constructions as necessary; refinement requires openness, unlike contraction that blocks it, while tacit knowledge can retain open loops and diverse policies. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:45](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L45).

- [x] **5.3-I28 · mechanism:** Love/safety desires route through deep permanence/selfhood priors and costly error; loosening priors reduces pressure, but indiscriminate lowering/disengagement can yield apathy not equanimity. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:51](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L51).

- [x] **5.3-I30 · mechanism:** Obtaining a proxy can leave deeper impossible permanence/selfhood targets unsatisfied, explaining disappointment and hedonic treadmill; objects remain reliable causes/routes, not collapsed away. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:53](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L53).
- [x] **5.3-I31 · claim:** Unmet deep desire registers high friction because dependent structures need recomputation; equanimity holds configurations reachable without pressure while retaining direction. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:53](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L53).
- [x] **5.3-I32 · definition:** Equanimity keeps targets/actions available but noncompulsory; low-weight difference demands prevent long-run latched contraction and preserve many states, permitting intensity rather than merely calmness. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:55](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L55).
- [x] **5.3-I33 · caveat:** Inflating predicted costs is not free: physiology constrains baseline, fake expectations lose surprise, and genuinely costly-world priors may lower tone/increase threat rather than wellbeing. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:57](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L57).
- [x] **5.3-I34 · claim:** Reducing hurt does not guarantee ending craving and may increase it by making wanted states salient absent skills. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:57](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L57).
- [x] **5.3-I35 · definition:** Mind contraction rigidly holds representations, distinguished from haptic body contraction. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:59](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L59).
- [x] **5.3-I36 · mechanism:** Acquired stable conditions adapt into baseline, but releasing contraction ends continuously paid capacity cost; unnoticed headache relief illustrates durable benefit visible through comparison/relapse. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:59](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L59).
- [x] **5.3-I37 · claim:** Equanimity handles old adverse conditions without repertoire closure; baseline rise can lower craving, increase slack/reachability/fluidity, and stop somatic confirmation; changes are lasting yet unremarkable. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:59](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L59).
- [x] **5.3-I38 · mechanism:** Decomposing solid hurt/anxiety and suffering gestalts reveals more non-maintaining policies; emptiness recognizes constructions as active process and liberates phenomenologically/epistemically. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:61](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L61).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Suffering is negatively felt organization around high-weight demand for a different maintained state with hard-to-reach relief; technical wanting differs from ordinary sustained usage, with cessation and practical reduction qualifications.
- First-order hurt is not suffering by itself; taṇhā gives rise to second-arrow craving/aversion as co-arising target-gap control directions.
- Maintenance couples contraction, unpleasant confirming evidence and renewed control, creating trigger-independent latches and rumination/epistemic-defence attractors when reachability/slack are low.
- Direction can remain wholesome without compulsory pressure; equanimity preserves actions and intensity without latching, unlike apathy, and desired objects proxy field configurations without losing causal reliability.
- Faking expected costs fails or trains threat; durable contraction release removes ongoing cost rather than a new pleasure adapting away, while emptiness deconstructs rigid gestalts to open alternative policies.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- First-order hurt becomes suffering when a highly weighted demand organizes the field around changing or maintaining experience; craving/aversion co-arise around target error.
- Low reachability, insufficient slack, somatic contraction, and repeated control failures maintain suffering, eventually latching into habitual attractors.
- The same defense loop can preserve epistemic ignorance; reducing target pressure need not remove wholesome direction, though indiscriminate disengagement risks apathy.
- Desired objects proxy predicted field configurations; equanimity preserves alternatives and prevents compulsory contraction without extinguishing targets.
- Releasing ongoing contraction costs can durably free capacity and raise baseline tone; emptiness investigates apparently solid suffering into mutable processes.

### 6.1 — Emptiness & Ontological Authority

- [x] **6.1-I01 · definition:** Burbea’s two exact quotations define emptiness as absent inherent/independent existence; identity/properties depend on conditions, relations, and field organization. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:1](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L1).
- [x] **6.1-I02 · example:** Optical illusion supports multiple valid conditioned organizations none inherent in lines; mood/conditioning recognition is a simple form and deep practice addresses suffering. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:1](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L1).
- [x] **6.1-I03 · claim:** Emptiness is model negative space/other side, not added component: compression, chunks, field, gestalts, dependency tree explain construction while emptiness recognizes it. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:10](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L10); [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:12](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L12); [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:16](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L16).
- [x] **6.1-I04 · mechanism:** Constructive sequence: priors organize features/relations, relations compress into chunks, chunks package gestalt, precision stabilizes usability. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:12](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L12).
- [x] **6.1-I05 · mechanism:** Emptiness reverses construction by examining solid gestalt, dechunking relations/parts, and tracing conditions. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:14](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L14); [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:16](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L16).
- [x] **6.1-I06 · caveat:** Chair depends on shape/context/knowledge and prior weighting like duck-rabbit/unreadable image; emptiness is not nihilism: chair remains physically real/useful at its level. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:18](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L18).
- [x] **6.1-I07 · definition:** Ontological authority is degree further inference depends on emergent gestalt, unlike precision strength; clear chair/dog appearance need not govern dependencies like all dogs dangerous and clenching. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:20](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L20); [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:22](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L22).
- [x] **6.1-I08 · mechanism:** Unsafe authority assimilates ambiguity and downweights safety, potentially deepening attractor basin; only some priors need authority and high authority need not yield wide/deep basin outside narrow context. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:22](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L22).
- [x] **6.1-I09 · example:** Limited chair authority permits sitting or reinterpreting as step/sculpture/wood without chairness governing everything. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:22](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L22).
- [x] **6.1-I10 · definition:** Reification concerns construction appearing independently real; authority concerns its use governing perception/action; real-making (e.g. religion) increases authority. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:22](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L22).
- [x] **6.1-I11 · mechanism:** Authority propagates violations across dependent priors and raises recomputation cost; depth tracks dependence across contexts, while low authority drops cheaply. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:24](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L24); [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:32](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L32).
- [x] **6.1-I12 · mechanism:** Authority self-maintains by cheap confirming integration and explaining away counter-friction; resonance/friction reinforce authority rather than establish accuracy. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:24](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L24).
- [x] **6.1-I13 · caveat:** Open truth-making loops expose unpredictable external feedback unlike real-making; lowering authority restores pruned branches, requires slack, and tends to fail when slack is low. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:24](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L24).
- [x] **6.1-I14 · claim:** Rigid/latched contraction may be authority in bodily substrate; interoceptive evidence confirms predicted reality and deep heavily weighted priors overpower simple safety representations. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:26](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L26).
- [x] **6.1-I15 · definition:** Latch is contraction held long enough to rigidify, acting as sticky embodied premise without recomputing; keeping alternative branches initially costs resources/slack. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:26](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L26).
- [x] **6.1-I16 · mechanism:** Inspection requires objectifying embodied gestalt into representation/notation; authoritative structuring premises resist becoming objects because they generate compression. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:28](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L28).
- [x] **6.1-I17 · example:** Chair is easy to decompose unlike highly dependent self; native grammar hides as judgment-generating structure and must be objectified into notation/refinement. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:28](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L28).
- [x] **6.1-I18 · definition:** Precision is current confidence; authority is further organizing use; depth is cross-context dependence; reification is invisible inherent appearance; sequence links stabilization, use, accumulated dependence, invisibility. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:20](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L20); [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:22](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L22); [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:30](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L30); [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:32](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L32).

- [x] **6.1-I21 · mechanism:** Emptiness lowers conditioned authority without preventing gestalts; widened reachable configurations can reduce suffering and improve intelligence. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:40](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L40).
- [x] **6.1-I22 · claim:** Because objects proxy configurations (5.3), emptiness directly addresses lasting target properties via alternate routes or releases pressure on permanence/selfhood priors. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:40](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L40).

- [x] **6.1-I24 · caveat:** Survival requires structured sensory input and some high authority; question is which things, how much, not abolishing authority generally. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:42](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L42).
- [x] **6.1-I25 · caveat:** Protected frontmatter scaffolding: unfinished summary placeholder and draft marker must remain exactly; do not supply missing summary. — [sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md:1](sections/emptiness-and-ontological-authority.md#L1).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Emptiness is absence of inherent independent existence, not nihilism; it reverses gestalt construction to conditions while preserving chairs’ practical reality.
- Ontological authority is downstream inference dependence, distinct from precision, reification and cross-context depth; clear appearances can have limited authority without deep/wide basins.
- High-authority premises cheaply assimilate confirming ambiguity and reject counter-input, raise revision costs, and need slack to reopen alternatives; somatic latches reinforce this.
- Hidden generating structure such as self/grammar/categories is harder to objectify than ordinary objects; Grothendieck illustrates inherited mathematical-frame exclusion.
- Emptiness makes configuration targets directly addressable by alternative routes; constructional transparency keeps useful gestalts alive without rigidity, asking which authority fits which context.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Emptiness denies independent inherent identity, not practical physical reality; it examines construction in reverse rather than adding a model component.
- Ontological authority concerns downstream use of a gestalt, unlike prediction precision or reified appearance; authority raises dependency depth and revision costs.
- Authority self-confirms through cheap integration and somatic latching, while lowering it needs slack and objectifying hidden structuring premises.
- Questioning authoritative categories enables novel discovery; emptiness keeps useful gestalts available while opening alternative routes to target configurations.
- Constructional transparency maintains recognition of conditioned construction without abolishing necessary authority or survival organization.

### 6.2 — Transparency

- [x] **6.2-I01 · definition:** Transparency is construction remaining apparent on all three compression layers while a gestalt is experienced and used; unlike phenomenal transparency, it is not invisible model construction. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:1](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L1).
- [x] **6.2-I02 · mechanism:** High ontological authority hides construction because the field runs on the gestalt; reification takes construction as inherent reality. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:1](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L1).
- [x] **6.2-I03 · caveat:** Transparency does not eliminate structure needed for life; it opens otherwise unavailable interpretations and dependency-tree branches. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:1](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L1); [sections/transparency-6-2.md:14](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L14).
- [x] **6.2-I04 · mechanism:** Precision moves from whole gestalt to parts, separating organizational usefulness from high ontological authority and reducing contraction. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:10](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L10).
- [x] **6.2-I05 · example:** A transparent dog gestalt still organizes legs and fur and supports instance-specific danger response without generalizing danger to every dog. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:10](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L10).
- [x] **6.2-I06 · example:** Deconstructing I am unsafe into a fast heartbeat redirects precision away from the suffering-producing label, though deep bodily contraction may feed confirming evidence back. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:10](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L10).
- [x] **6.2-I07 · claim:** After sufficient practice, separately addressable parts prune the tree so integration stays equally cheap or cheaper while authority falls. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:12](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L12).
- [x] **6.2-I08 · caveat:** Initial part-by-part transparency can be computationally expensive and fail under tiredness or low slack, returning old patterns; training is required. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:12](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L12).
- [x] **6.2-I09 · mechanism:** Transparency operationalizes inverse construction: de-chunk gestalts into relations and trace conditions; reduced reification separately addresses components and opens branches. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:14](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L14).
- [x] **6.2-I10 · caveat:** Seeing a high-authority gestalt has perspectival limits like seeing one’s eyes; alternatives cost attention and require slack, especially under fatigue or threat. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:16](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L16).
- [x] **6.2-I11 · definition:** Slack has two senses: spare resources to leave a stuck state and the condition governing whether the field latches initially. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:18](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L18).
- [x] **6.2-I12 · example:** Sleep and unclenching add capacity, whereas transparency and equanimity reduce latching: a rested critic can remain stuck while a tired equanimous person lets criticism drop. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:18](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L18).
- [x] **6.2-I13 · mechanism:** Releasing contraction frees formerly wasted capacity rather than merely becoming background pleasure; rest alone does not change recurrent latching. — [sections/the-suffering-loop.md:59](sections/the-suffering-loop.md#L59).
- [x] **6.2-I14 · caveat:** Insight alone cannot maintain change under extreme depletion; equanimity keeps competing branches open but exhaustion can reactivate residual latched patterns. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:18](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L18).
- [x] **6.2-I15 · caveat:** Knowing how to de-chunk anxiety does not make it affordable in a deep basin; trunk dependencies raise costs and context can preserve authority, requiring repeated refinement passes. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:20](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L20); [sections/transparency-6-2.md:22](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L22).
- [x] **6.2-I16 · example:** Anxiety can be targeted as scary sensations, deeper sensations happening to me, or trunk thing-ness/innate priors; deeper transparency affects more contexts but costs more. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:22](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L22).
- [x] **6.2-I17 · caveat:** Whether outer-branch practice directly loosens deeper structures or builds capacity for direct deep work is explicitly unclear. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:22](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L22).
- [x] **6.2-I18 · mechanism:** Trunk contraction embodies high authority; unchanged interoceptive evidence can regenerate reification after epistemic insight, so tension release and transparency lower reinforcement together. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:24](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L24).
- [x] **6.2-I19 · caveat:** Long-held habitual contraction narrows possible patterns: release and restoration then diverge, and exact duration is unknown. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:24](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L24).
- [x] **6.2-I20 · claim:** Speculatively, deep haptic contraction needs outside resources to escape its basin; psychedelics, heat, and sustained attention are proposed to enable lasting release similarly. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:24](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L24).
- [x] **6.2-I21 · example:** A short cast permits immediate movement after removal; prolonged casting changes tissue and requires rehabilitation, illustrating release versus restored patterns. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:24](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L24).
- [x] **6.2-I22 · mechanism:** A collapsed tree can leave alternative states bodily unreachable even after seeing through a prior; small repetitions with slack restore tolerable states, while equanimity prevents new collapse. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:24](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L24).
- [x] **6.2-I23 · definition:** Sensory clarity is proposed as embodied transparency: resolving a sensation gestalt into changing lower-level sensations. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:26](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L26).
- [x] **6.2-I24 · mechanism:** Lower sensation authority requires less field organization, reducing ongoing integration cost and negative felt tone in energetic-triad terms. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:26](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L26).
- [x] **6.2-I25 · mechanism:** High-authority first-order hurt and should-not-happen beliefs constrain policy to craving/aversion; transparency reduces integration cost and opens distinct action branches. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:28](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L28).
- [x] **6.2-I26 · mechanism:** Memory reinstates context-dependent configurations and somatics; without reinstated defensive contraction, sensations can recur without running the self-confirming suffering loop. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:28](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L28).
- [x] **6.2-I27 · mechanism:** Authority reduction keeps competing paths active, weakens basin depth/width and entry-exit asymmetry, increasing reachability without eliminating environmental, attentional, or bodily reinstatement. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:30](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L30).
- [x] **6.2-I28 · example:** Seeing this anxiety is dangerous as constructed may let this frustration is unbearable replace the same shallow position without deep reorganization. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:32](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L32).
- [x] **6.2-I29 · caveat:** Shallow insight may propagate more slowly toward solid-sensation priors; deep generative targets can be faster but require slack and steady pacing to avoid destabilization. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:32](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L32).
- [x] **6.2-I30 · definition:** Thing-ness construction is proposed as segmentation into bounded units, unit coherence, and persistence across seemingly continuous time. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:34](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L34).
- [x] **6.2-I31 · claim:** Thing-ness is tentatively the deepest practical non-destabilizing target; lowering it can undercut suffering and craving/aversion substrates depended on by almost every other gestalt. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:34](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L34).
- [x] **6.2-I32 · mechanism:** Transparent thing-ness lowers integration cost and update propagation, shrinking felt-tone swings and altering resonance/friction because fewer structures absorb surprise. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:36](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L36).
- [x] **6.2-I33 · mechanism:** Baseline tone rises from fewer reified-resource costs and impossible targets such as permanence; situations become moving parts and felt shifts gain pathways. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:36](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L36).

- [x] **6.2-I35 · caveat:** Seeing a model as constructed does not make it true; open refinement loops remain necessary. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:40](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L40).
- [x] **6.2-I36 · mechanism:** Transparency reduces defensive epistemic suffering and enables objectification of high-authority structures; inspecting constituent priors allows deeper updates and interrupts contraction self-maintenance. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:40](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L40).
- [x] **6.2-I37 · claim:** Sensory blurriness regenerates the high-authority substrate of suffering; differentiated conditions parallel sensory clarity. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:40](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L40); [sections/transparency-6-2.md:26](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L26).

- [x] **6.2-I39 · claim:** DP/DR is tentatively linked to insufficient slack; surrender/releasing contraction may stop resource expenditure demanding a different state and sustaining it. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:42](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L42).
- [x] **6.2-I40 · caveat:** Transparency need not hold all alternatives open or paralyze action: useful compression enables firm commitment while low authority permits contextual updates. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:44](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L44).
- [x] **6.2-I41 · example:** Flat-earther rigidity illustrates firm action without truth-oriented refinement; age-related rigid models are offered as a common parallel. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:44](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L44).
- [x] **6.2-I42 · claim:** The target is not authority itself but authority the system cannot clearly see and drop; the question becomes what deserves authority when. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:46](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L46).
- [x] **6.2-I43 · example:** Children’s play, adult frames, and phenomenological safety practice illustrate treating a thing as given then dropping it without contraction. — [sections/transparency-6-2.md:46](sections/transparency-6-2.md#L46).

All five previously recorded compressed takeaways still match the original: transparency versus phenomenal transparency, trained/contextual precision, escape versus nonlatching, depth/rehabilitation, and fallible but revisable commitment remain. The transition from tired equanimous critique handling to rest versus insight remains grammatical and motivated. The deleted ongoing-cost retrospective is preserved exactly in meaning in 5.3.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Constructional transparency keeps generating processes apparent while using a gestalt, opposite phenomenal transparency; weighting present parts can reduce overgeneralized authority without removing a dog or danger response.
- Training and slack are required initially, with context/fatigue failures; escape capacity differs from equanimity/transparency preventing latching, and rest alone does not change habitual capture.
- Prior depth predicts difficulty and generality; old somatic latches require both release and separate rehabilitation, illustrated by a cast, with energy/intervention mechanisms explicitly speculative.
- Transparent sensations/memories open policies and weaken but do not erase basins; shallow replacements motivate gradual deeper thingness work, supported by Thisdell attribution.
- Transparency lowers defended costs and aids but does not prove correct models; DP/DR interpretation is uncertain, and wise commitment remains firm and revisable without holding all branches open.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Transparency exposes construction without abolishing useful gestalts or decisive action.
- Redistributing precision to constituent parts can lower authority, contraction, suffering, and update barriers while retaining cheap integration after training.
- Depth, bodily reinforcement, context, and slack govern difficulty and generality; long-held contraction may require separate rehabilitation of lost reachability.
- Thing-ness is proposed as a deep practical target, but deep work requires sufficient slack and gradual pacing.
- Transparency is not truth or unlimited flexibility: open refinement loops, embodied capacity, and revisable authority remain necessary.

### 7.1 — Sanity & Wisdom

- [x] **7.1-I01 · claim:** Given constructed gestalts can in principle be transparent like optical illusions, the next question is wise use and direction, not merely insight. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:1](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L1).
- [x] **7.1-I02 · definition:** Sanity means enough compression to act, enough transparency to update, and moving between abstraction levels as circumstances require. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:10](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L10).
- [x] **7.1-I03 · definition:** Wisdom is sane flexibility oriented toward targets worth weighting and consequential actions; sanity is prerequisite because unseen field structure limits free target selection. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:10](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L10).
- [x] **7.1-I04 · citation:** Korzybski defines consciousness of abstracting as awareness of characteristics omitted by abstraction. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:12](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L12).
- [x] **7.1-I05 · citation:** Thisdell describes sanity as lucid mobility up and down abstraction, recognizes chunking, and urges both zero-level detail and expanding higher capacity before incomprehensible infinity. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:15](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L15).
- [x] **7.1-I06 · example:** Thisdell’s all-is-one speaker cannot handle dinner shopping, taxes, and politics because metaphysical abstraction mismatches practical demands. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:15](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L15).
- [x] **7.1-I07 · example:** Forest, trees, ground dirt, and canopy illustrate different needed scales and confusion from remaining fixed at one. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:15](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L15).
- [x] **7.1-I08 · definition:** The abstraction ladder is a continuous experiential hierarchy interfacing dependency-tree levels; ascending compresses details, descending decompresses working parts. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:31](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L31).
- [x] **7.1-I09 · mechanism:** Compression provides strategic orientation, decompression specific actions; neither is superior and goals illustrate their complementarity. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:31](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L31).
- [x] **7.1-I10 · caveat:** Lost fluidity produces confusion and suffering from mismatched scale, such as detail overload when overview is needed. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:31](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L31).
- [x] **7.1-I11 · mechanism:** A sufficiently structuring gestalt can absorb counter-evidence as confirmation; flexibility within this closed field produces internal resonance without correspondence. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:33](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L33).
- [x] **7.1-I12 · claim:** Sanity requires open loops with constraints the field cannot generate; it fails through fixed-scale contraction or unconstrained flexibility rewarded only by resonance. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:33](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L33).
- [x] **7.1-I13 · mechanism:** Children lacking adult compressions use available animate/relational priors to construct event representations. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:35](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L35).
- [x] **7.1-I14 · example:** A child imagines a river choosing whether rocks skip as a tiny game or answers to questions; the full monologue illustrates animate metaphor. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:39](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L39).
- [x] **7.1-I15 · claim:** The child’s inaccurate but vivid river metaphor spans fit-distance from felt event to embodied priors; adult angles/spin gestalts are factually correct but described as dead. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:47](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L47).
- [x] **7.1-I16 · citation:** Vacha’s Wise Old Child ideal calls for mature childlike wonder; Michael Pollan’s trip quote links psychedelic childlike sensitivity to undoing adult environmental exclusion. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:49](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L49).
- [x] **7.1-I17 · mechanism:** Adult conditioning makes cheap stable reified chunks feel dull; contemplation and loosened deep priors restore newness by expanding rather than discarding competent priors. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:49](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L49).
- [x] **7.1-I18 · claim:** Childlike liveliness does not itself confer wisdom; orientation to moment-appropriate targets is missing. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:51](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L51).
- [x] **7.1-I19 · example:** A conman can update and read situations sanely while pursuing scams; flexibility cannot alone select worthy outcomes. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:53](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L53).
- [x] **7.1-I20 · caveat:** Wisdom seems nonalgorithmic and ineffable, more describable by removing obstructive contraction than installing a correct rule/model; dropping some contraction does not guarantee it. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:55](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L55).
- [x] **7.1-I21 · example:** Ethically scandalous spiritual leaders illustrate residual blockage despite released contraction; the example ends in an unfinished sentence. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:55](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L55).
- [x] **7.1-I22 · mechanism:** Targets cannot be removed: wisdom preserves direction but drops second-order control pressure so action is decisive without compulsory policy. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:57](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L57).
- [x] **7.1-I23 · definition:** Equanimity preserves available targets/actions without felt necessity; apathy instead reduces field salience so nothing is worth targeting. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:57](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L57).
- [x] **7.1-I24 · mechanism:** Practical transparent refinement identifies a constructed gestalt and its scale, dependencies, blocked alternatives, then revises precision, model, targets, or policies and repeats. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:59](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L59).
- [x] **7.1-I25 · claim:** There is no global endpoint: transparent structures become default and can themselves be objectified; priors are reinstantiated moment-to-moment rather than discarded, expanding reachable interpretations/actions. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:59](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L59).
- [x] **7.1-I26 · caveat:** Resonance/friction and felt senses can mislead; integration ease within a field is not correspondence, and sanity/wisdom seek feedback and flexibility rather than a singular final map. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:61](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L61); [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:33](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L33).
- [x] **7.1-I27 · citation:** Grothendieck criticizes texts presenting immutable research results while hiding the actual natural research process and reinforcing credentialized knowledge. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:65](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L65).
- [x] **7.1-I28 · citation:** Grothendieck describes research as passionate childlike questioning and listening, a universal gift rather than a privileged scholarly activity. — [sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md:65](sections/sanity-and-wisdom.md#L65).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Independent first reading retains the original-level takeaways. Specific mechanisms, argument examples and caveats are separately resolved under every inventory ID; existing source problems remain flags, not compression failures.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Sanity combines actionable compression, transparent updating and abstraction mobility; wisdom directs that sane flexibility toward worthwhile targets and requires it.
- Korzybski/Thisdell illustrate lucid abstraction climbing, with neither detail nor summary inherently superior and external constraints needed beyond flexible self-confirmation.
- Child river stories and wise-old-child/Pollan imagery contrast alive construction with mature competence; releasing reification preserves competence rather than becoming naïve.
- Wisdom is partly inexpressible/known by negation but release does not guarantee ethics, as conmen/spiritual scandals show; direction without pressure differs from apathy.
- Transparent refinement recursively objectifies its own new defaults without endpoint; felt senses stay fallible and maps can be useful/accurate without being territory.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Sanity combines actionable compression, transparency for updating, and mobility across abstraction levels; wisdom orients this capacity toward worthwhile targets.
- Neither rigid abstraction nor flexible self-confirming resonance is sane without externally constrained open loops.
- Mature childlike wonder retains competence while holding conditioned priors lightly.
- Wisdom preserves target direction while dropping coercive pressure; it is not apathy or an installable final algorithm.
- Transparent iterative refinement has no global endpoint and treats resonance/friction as fallible signals rather than truth.

### 7.2 — Practice

- [x] **7.2-I01 · citation:** Aristotle Nicomachean Ethics says builders, lyreplayers, and virtuous people become so by doing corresponding acts. — [sections/practice.md:1](sections/practice.md#L1).

- [x] **7.2-I03 · mechanism:** Compressed histories of perception, attention, belief, action, and feedback shape future interpretation; practice controllably molds the field and supports self-authorship. — [sections/practice.md:14](sections/practice.md#L14). Sources: [https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.02688/full](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.02688/full).
- [x] **7.2-I04 · citation:** Reasoning and internal/external representations enlarge living systems’ cognitive light cone, linked to a Frontiers article. — [sections/practice.md:14](sections/practice.md#L14). Sources: [https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.02688/full](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.02688/full).
- [x] **7.2-I05 · claim:** Wisdom, transparency, and nonhabitual contraction must themselves be practiced rather than merely understood. — [sections/practice.md:14](sections/practice.md#L14). Sources: [https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.02688/full](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.02688/full).
- [x] **7.2-I06 · definition:** Practice is repeated engagement directing attention/awareness across spatial and temporal levels, using feedback to retune field dispositions and dynamics toward targets. — [sections/practice.md:16](sections/practice.md#L16).
- [x] **7.2-I07 · caveat:** Precision weighting, mood, and context vary influence: a few highly weighted moves can exceed many weak repetitions, so equal practice amounts yield unequal results. — [sections/practice.md:16](sections/practice.md#L16).
- [x] **7.2-I08 · mechanism:** Practice operates across immediate availability, dwelling, repeated deeper-prior change, and offline sleep consolidation; insight alone may not change default state-generating dispositions. — [sections/practice.md:16](sections/practice.md#L16).
- [x] **7.2-I09 · mechanism:** Notation changes quickly, interacting models more slowly, and embodied/deep-prior knowledge slowest; contradiction may revise wording before tacit knowledge, causing field dissonance. — [sections/practice.md:18](sections/practice.md#L18).
- [x] **7.2-I10 · mechanism:** Corrective practice requires slack to hold competing error branches instead of habitual active inference; low slack reenacts habits rather than exploration. — [sections/practice.md:20](sections/practice.md#L20).
- [x] **7.2-I11 · caveat:** Practice capacity is task/configuration-specific and must be reachable during practice; anxiety/social pressure can hide available skills. — [sections/practice.md:20](sections/practice.md#L20).
- [x] **7.2-I12 · example:** Routines, rituals, external notation, and supportive friends preserve capacity by reducing uncertainty; volatile environments require more slack. — [sections/practice.md:20](sections/practice.md#L20).
- [x] **7.2-I13 · caveat:** Too few constraints produce unwieldy search, too many force premature collapse; good practice navigates structure while retaining exploration capacity. — [sections/practice.md:20](sections/practice.md#L20).
- [x] **7.2-I14 · mechanism:** Practice cycle: felt understanding → testable probe → test against field/external structure → notice cost signals/baseline → locate cause → update representation/notation → repeat. — [sections/practice.md:24](sections/practice.md#L24).
- [x] **7.2-I15 · caveat:** Embodied recognition cannot be forced; sufficiently weighted practice produces it, while notation/representations stabilize implicit material for objectification and revision. — [sections/practice.md:26](sections/practice.md#L26).
- [x] **7.2-I16 · example:** Conversational skill words stabilize a felt sense; actual probes expose habitual interpretations taking over when deliberate weighting is absent. — [sections/practice.md:26](sections/practice.md#L26).
- [x] **7.2-I17 · caveat:** Not every practice follows the exact stated refinement loop, though its general change mechanisms apply. — [sections/practice.md:26](sections/practice.md#L26).
- [x] **7.2-I18 · caveat:** Resonance/friction measure integration-cost surprise, not truth or moral rightness; familiar false ideas can resonate and true ones cause friction. — [sections/practice.md:28](sections/practice.md#L28).
- [x] **7.2-I19 · mechanism:** Signals can originate in any compression layer, external evidence, or low slack; practice must diagnose violated/satisfied structure before updating. — [sections/practice.md:28](sections/practice.md#L28).
- [x] **7.2-I20 · mechanism:** Open loops add feedback the practicing field cannot generate itself, preventing self-reinforcing repetition and making new dependency branches available. — [sections/practice.md:28](sections/practice.md#L28).
- [x] **7.2-I21 · mechanism:** Weighted recurrent relations and successful coactivated priors compress into automatic chunks, reducing separate integration and compounding future search savings. — [sections/practice.md:30](sections/practice.md#L30).
- [x] **7.2-I22 · example:** Math chunks permit harder problems and expanded temporal/spatial targets rather than merely leaving capacity idle. — [sections/practice.md:30](sections/practice.md#L30).
- [x] **7.2-I23 · caveat:** Predictably cheap practiced chunks become baseline and cease obvious resonance/friction; fluency is neither new learning nor proof of accuracy. — [sections/practice.md:30](sections/practice.md#L30).
- [x] **7.2-I24 · mechanism:** Repetition cheapens a chunk but also expands dependencies and ontological authority, making inaccurate entrenched chunks costly to relearn. — [sections/practice.md:30](sections/practice.md#L30).
- [x] **7.2-I25 · citation:** Deliberate practice is cited as mindful performance repetition just beyond capability, requiring slack rather than ease alone. — [sections/practice.md:32](sections/practice.md#L32). Sources: [https://www.justinmath.com/files/advice-on-upskilling.pdf](https://www.justinmath.com/files/advice-on-upskilling.pdf).
- [x] **7.2-I26 · caveat:** Difficulty alone can train threat, helplessness, or frozen action; frictionless practice can deepen automaticity without refinement, with suitable depth depending on targets. — [sections/practice.md:32](sections/practice.md#L32). - [x] **7.2-I39 · mechanism:** Sanity supplies revisable transparency and flexibility, wisdom supplies worthwhile targets; wisdom practice requires ongoing reorientation, decisive action without defense, and openness without losing direction. — [sections/practice.md:40](sections/practice.md#L40).

**Initial reviewer result: reported matched.** Re-read the entire final 7.2 after the exact back-reference deletion. All five previously recorded compressed takeaways still match the original. The paragraph now moves directly from low-slack habitual repetition to anxiety/social-pressure access, which concretely establishes configuration specificity before requiring capacity during practice. The canonical relational definition remains in 2.3; no referent or transition gap appears.

Compressed first-time-reader takeaways:

- Enacted precision-weighted practice selectively authors learned field dispositions; moment weighting, dwelling, repetition and offline consolidation act at different speeds, unlike insight alone.
- Corrective practice needs reachable task-specific slack, supportive structure and moderate constraints; inspectable probes/diagnosis and open feedback stop resonance-guided repetition becoming defence or delusion.
- Chunks free capacity but may deepen inaccurate authority; deliberate edge practice differs from difficulty alone and needed automaticity.
- Durable memory differs from cue-dependent access; transfer removes unavailable supports conditionally, establishes prerequisites/fundamentals and enables expert fine discrimination.
- Concrete varied examples build relational embodiment/SPT generalization beyond rote definitions; practice is ethically nonneutral and wisdom remains a revisable orientation, not an installed finished model.

Original-reader takeaways used for comparison:

- Practice selectively retunes the field through weighted repeated engagement and feedback, not thought or repetition alone.
- Knowledge layers update at different rates; durable embodied change needs appropriate weighting, reachable slack, and context-sensitive training.
- Open-loop feedback diagnoses fallible resonance/friction while chunking lowers cost and increases both competence and entrenchment risk.
- Memory is dispositional reconstruction; prerequisites, varied concrete examples, invariants, and removed temporary support enable generalizable expertise.
- Practice trains ethical or harmful dispositions alike; wisdom supplies revisable direction without contractive defense.

This checklist records inventory coverage and initial reader findings; it does not prove that every local reference, explanation, or qualification survived compression, or that the claims are true. The exact-source restorations below address the user-identified losses. Unresolved source errors are recorded in [flags.md](flags.md); current counts and verification are in [change-log.md](change-log.md).
